Before one of these chairs, their toes pointing upwards against the fender, were a pair of slippers; on a table close by stood an old lead tobacco-box, flanked by a churchwarden pipe, a spirit decanter, a glass, and a plate on which were set out sugar and lemon﻿—these Brereton took to be indicative that Kitely, his evening constitutional over, was in the habit of taking a quiet pipe and a glass of something warm before going to bed. And looking round still further he became aware of an open door﻿—the door into which Miss Pett had withdrawn﻿—and of a bed within on which Kitely now lay, with Dr. Rockcliffe and the police-sergeant bending over him. The other policemen stood by the table in the living-room, and one of them﻿—the man who had picked up the pocketbook﻿—whispered audibly to Cotherstone as he and his companions entered.

“The doctor’s taking it off him,” he said, with a meaning nod of his head. “I’ll lay aught it’s as I say, Mr. Cotherstone.”

“Looks like it,” agreed Cotherstone, rubbing his hands. “It certainly looks like it, George. Sharp of you to notice it, though.”

===Chinese===

三人走进小屋的起居室——这是间宽敞的老式屋子，椽子裸露在外，屋里烧着一炉旺火，炉旁两侧各摆着一把舒适的安乐椅。其中一把椅子跟前放着一双拖鞋，鞋尖朝上抵着炉围；近旁一张桌上立着一只旧铅皮烟丝盒，边上搁着一支长杆烟斗、一只烈酒瓶、一只玻璃杯，还有一只盛着糖和柠檬的盘子——布里尔顿心想，这些东西表明，基特利晚间散步归来，素有睡前安安静静抽一斗烟、喝杯热酒的习惯。再往四下里看，他又注意到一扇敞开的门——佩特小姐方才退进去的那扇——门里一张床，基特利此刻就躺在上面，罗克利夫医生和警佐正俯身查看。另外几名警察站在起居室的桌旁，其中一人——就是捡起那只皮夹的那位——等他和同伴们进来，便压低嗓门跟科瑟斯通说话，声音却人人听得见。

“大夫正从他身上往下取那玩意儿呢，”他说着，意味深长地点了点头。“我拿啥打赌都行，准是像我说的一样，科瑟斯通先生。”

“看样子是，”科瑟斯通搓着手表示同意。“确实像那么回事，乔治。亏你眼尖，留意到了。”

===Original===

Brereton took this conversation to refer to the mysterious clue, and his suspicion was confirmed a moment later. The doctor and the sergeant came into the living-room, the doctor carrying something in his hand which he laid down on the centre table in full view of all of them. And Brereton saw then that he had removed from the dead man’s neck the length of grey cord with which he had been strangled.

There was something exceedingly sinister in the mere placing of that cord before the eyes of these living men. It had wrought the death of another man, who, an hour before, had been as full of vigorous life as themselves; some man, equally vigorous, had used it as the instrument of a foul murder. Insignificant in itself, a mere piece of strongly spun and twisted hemp, it was yet singularly suggestive﻿—one man, at any rate, amongst those who stood looking at it, was reminded by it that the murderer who had used it must even now have the fear of another and a stronger cord before him.

“Find who that cord belongs to, and you may get at something,” suddenly observed the doctor, glancing at the policemen. “You say it’s a butcher’s cord?”

The man who had just whispered to Cotherstone nodded.

===Chinese===

布里尔顿断定这段话说的就是那条神秘的线索，片刻之后，他的猜想便得到了证实。医生和警佐走进起居室，医生手里拿着件东西，放在屋子中央的桌上，当着众人的面。布里尔顿这才看清，那是从死者脖颈上取下来的一截灰色绳索——基特利就是被它勒死的。

把这根绳索往这些活人眼前一放，本身就透着一股说不出的凶邪。它断送了另一个人的性命——而一个钟头之前，那人还同他们一样精力充沛地活着；某个同样身强力壮的人，把它用作了肮脏谋杀的凶器。这东西本身微不足道，不过是一段捻得结结实实的麻绳，却格外发人深省——至少，站在那儿望着它的众人当中有一个，由它联想到：用过这根绳子的凶手，此刻心里必定还悬着对另一根更结实绳索的恐惧。

“查清这绳子是谁家的，兴许就能摸到门路，”医生忽然说道，眼睛扫了扫那几名警察。“你们说这是屠户的绳子？”

方才凑近科瑟斯通咬耳朵的那个警察点了点头。

===Original===

“It’s a pig-killer’s cord, sir,” he answered. “It’s what a pig-killer fastens the pig down with﻿—on the cratch.”

“A cratch?﻿—what’s that?” asked Brereton, who had gone close to the table to examine the cord, and had seen that, though slender, it was exceedingly strong, and of closely wrought fibre. “Is it a sort of hurdle?”

“That’s it, sir,” assented the policeman. “It is a sort of hurdle﻿—on four legs. They lay the pig on it, don’t you see, and tie it down with a cord of this sort﻿—this cord’s been used for that﻿—it’s greasy with long use.”

“And it has been cut off a longer piece, of course,” said the doctor. “These cords are of considerable length, aren’t they?”

“Good length, sir﻿—there’s a regular coil, like,” said the man. He, too, bent down and looked at the length before him. “This has been cut off what you might call recent,” he went on, pointing to one end. “And cut off with a sharp knife, too.”

===Chinese===

“这是宰猪人的绳子，长官，”他答道。“宰猪的使它把猪捆住——捆在宰架上。”

“宰架？——那是啥东西？”布里尔顿问。他已走到近前细看那根绳子，看出它虽细，却极其结实，纤维编得密密实实。“是栅栏架一类的吗？”

“正是，长官，”那警察附和道。“就是一种栅栏架——底下带四条腿。您瞧，他们把猪搁在上头，拿这种绳子把它捆牢——这根就是干这个使的——使久了，都磨出油性了。”

“而且这准是从长绳上割下来的一截，”医生说。“这种绳子原本都相当长，是不是？”

“长得足着呢，长官——通常都盘成实实在在的一卷，”那人说。他也俯下身，端详眼前这一截。“这一截割下来的工夫，您可以说是新的，”他接着说，指了指一头。“而且是用快刀割的。”

===Original===

The police sergeant glanced at the doctor as if asking advice on the subject of putting his thoughts into words.

“Well?” said the doctor, with a nod of assent. “Of course, you’ve got something in your mind, sergeant?”

“Well, there is a man who kills pigs, and has such cords as that, lives close by, doctor,” he answered. “You know who I mean﻿—the man they call Gentleman Jack.”

“You mean Harborough,” said the doctor. “Well﻿—you’d better ask him if he knows anything. Somebody might have stolen one of his cords. But there are other pig-killers in the town, of course.”

===Chinese===

警佐看了看医生，仿佛在请示该不该把自己的想法说出口。

“怎么？”医生点头示意他说下去。“你自然是有想法了，警佐？”

“是这样，有个人专干宰猪的营生，家里就有这样的绳子，住得离这儿很近，大夫，”他答道。“您知道我说的是谁——就是大伙儿叫他‘绅士杰克’（Gentleman Jack）的那位。”

“你是说哈伯勒（Harborough），”医生说。“好吧——你们最好去问问他知道些什么。没准儿是有人偷了他一根绳子。不过镇上自然还有别的宰猪人。”

===Original===

“Not on this side the town, there aren’t,” remarked another policeman.

“What is plain,” continued the doctor, looking at Cotherstone and the others, “is that Kitely was strangled by this rope, and that everything on him of any value was taken. You’d better find out what he had, or was likely to have, on him, sergeant. Ask the housekeeper.”

Miss Pett came from the inner room, where she had already begun her preparations for laying out the body. She was as calm as when Bent first told her of what had occurred, and she stood at the end of the table, the cord between her and her questioners, and showed no emotion, no surprise at what had occurred.

“Can you tell aught about this, ma’am?” asked the sergeant. “You see your master’s met his death at somebody’s hands, and there’s no doubt he’s been robbed, too. Do you happen to know what he had on him?”

===Chinese===

“镇子这一边可没有，”另一名警察搭话道。

“眼下明白的是，”医生接着说，目光扫向科瑟斯通和其他人，“基特利是叫这根绳子勒死的，他身上值钱的东西都叫人拿走了。你去查查他身上带了什么，或者多半会带什么，警佐。问问女管家。”

佩特小姐从里屋走了出来，她已在里头着手张罗给死者净身穿衣的事。她同本特最初把出事的消息告诉她时一样镇定，站在桌子一头，那根绳子横在她和问话人中间，对发生的一切既不动声色，也不见惊异。

“这个您能说出个啥来吗，太太？”警佐问。“您瞧，您主人是死在了旁人手里，而且八成还叫人劫了财。您可知道他身上带着些什么？”

===Original===

The housekeeper, who had her arms full of linen, set her burden down on a clotheshorse in front of the fire before she replied. She seemed to be thinking deeply, and when she turned round again, it was to shake her queerly ornamented head.

“Well, I couldn’t say exactly,” she answered. “But I shouldn’t wonder if it was a good deal﻿—for such as him, you know. He did carry money on him﻿—he was never short of money ever since I knew him, and sometimes he’d a fair amount in his pockets﻿—I know, of course, because he’d pull it out, loose gold, and silver, and copper, and I’ve seen him take banknotes out of his pocketbook. But he’d be very like to have a good deal more than usual on him tonight.”

“Why?” asked the sergeant.

“Because he’d been to the bank this morning to draw his pension money,” replied Miss Pett. “I don’t know how much that would be, any more than I know where it came from. He was a close man﻿—he’d never tell anybody more than he liked, and he never told me aught about that. But I do know it was what you’d call a fair amount﻿—for a man that lives in a cottage. He went to the bank this noon﻿—he always went once a quarter﻿—and he said this afternoon that he’d go and pay his rent to Mr. Cotherstone there﻿—”

===Chinese===

女管家怀里抱满了亚麻布，先把这一抱东西搁在火前的晾衣架上，这才开口作答。她像是在凝神细想，等再转过身来，摇了摇那颗装点得古里古怪的脑袋。

“唉，我可说不准，”她答道。“不过依我看，数目不会小——像他这号人，您知道的。他身上确实带钱——打我认识他起，他就从不缺钱，有时兜里还揣着好些——这我当然知道，因为他会把钱掏出来，散的金币啊、银币啊、铜子儿啊，我还见过他从皮夹里抽出钞票来。可今晚他身上带的，多半比往常还要多得多。”

“为什么？”警佐问。

“因为他今儿早上刚去银行取了他的年金，”佩特小姐答道。“取了多少我不知道，就像那钱打哪儿来的我也不知道一样。他是个嘴严的人——不乐意说的半个字不吐，这件事他从没跟我说过啥。可我确实知道，那是笔您称得上可观的数目——对住村舍的人来说。他今儿晌午去的银行——他向来一季去一回——今儿下午还说他要去把钱付给那边的科瑟斯通先生，付房租——”

===Original===

“As he did,” muttered Cotherstone, “yes﻿—he did that.”

“Well, he’d have all the rest of his money on him,” continued the housekeeper. “And he’d have what he had before, because he’d other money coming in than that pension. And I tell you he was the sort of man that carried his money about him﻿—he was foolish that way. And then he’d a very valuable watch and chain﻿—he told me they were a presentation, and cost nearly a hundred pounds. And of course, he’d a pocketbook full of papers.”

“This pocketbook?” asked the sergeant.

“Aye, that’s it, right enough,” assented Miss Pett. “But he always had it bursting with bits of letters and papers. You don’t mean to say you found it empty? You did?﻿—very well then, I’m no fool, and I say that if he’s been murdered, there’s been some reason for it altogether apart from robbing him of what money and things he had on him! Whoever’s taken his papers wanted ’em bad!”

===Chinese===

“他是付了，”科瑟斯通咕哝道，“是——这事他办了。”

“这么说，他其余的钱就都带在身上了，”女管家接着说。“原先攒下的那些也在，因为除了那份年金，他还有别的进项。我跟您说吧，他就是那种把钱全带在身上的人——这方面他糊涂。还有，他有块很值钱的怀表和表链——他跟我说那是人家送的礼，花了将近一百镑。当然啦，还有个塞满纸片文件的皮夹。”

“是这个皮夹吗？”警佐问。

“哎，就是它，错不了，”佩特小姐应道。“可那皮夹里向来塞满了信件纸片，都快撑破了。你们不会是说，捡到它的时候是空的吧？真是空的？——那好，我可不是傻子，我要说：他要真是叫人谋害的，那缘由就绝不单是图他身上的钱财物件！拿走他那些纸片的人，是非要它们不可！”

===Original===

“About his habits, now?” said the sergeant, ignoring Miss Pett’s suggestion. “Did he go walking on the Shawl every night?”

“Regular as clockwork,” answered the housekeeper. “He used to read and write a deal at night﻿—then he’d side away all his books and papers, get his supper, and go out for an hour, walking round and about. Then he’d come in, put on his slippers﻿—there they are, set down to warm for him﻿—smoke one pipe, drink one glass of toddy﻿—there’s the stuff for it﻿—and go to bed. He was the regularest man I ever knew, in all he did.”

“Was he out longer than usual tonight?” asked Bent, who saw that the sergeant had no more to ask. “You seemed to suggest that, when we came.”

“Well, he was a bit longer,” admitted Miss Pett. “Of course, he varied. But an hour was about his time. Up and down and about the hillside he’d go﻿—in and out of the coppices. I’ve warned him more than once.”

===Chinese===

“再说说他的日常习惯吧，”警佐说，不去理会佩特小姐的猜测。“他每天晚上都上肖山散步吗？”

“准得跟钟表一样，”女管家答道。“他夜里总要读不少书、写不少东西——然后把书啊纸啊统统收拾到一边，吃了晚饭，出门走上一个钟头，四处转转。回来就换上拖鞋——喏，就在那儿，给他烘着呢——抽一斗烟，喝一杯热酒——原料就摆在那儿——然后上床睡觉。他做什么都有定规，是我这辈子见过的最有定规的人。”

“今晚他出去得比平时久吗？”本特问，他看出警佐没有别的要问了。“我们方才来的时候，您像是这个意思。”

“嗯，是久了点儿，”佩特小姐承认道。“当然啦，他也不是回回一样。可一个钟头差不离是他的准数。他就在山坡上上下下、四下转悠——小树林子里钻进钻出。我不止一回提醒过他。”

===Original===

“But why?” asked Brereton, whose curiosity was impelling him to take a part in this drama. “What reason had you for warning him?”

Miss Pett turned and looked scrutinizingly at her last questioner. She took a calm and close observation of him and her curious face relaxed into something like a smile.

“I can tell what you are, mister,” she said. “A law gentleman! I’ve seen your sort many a time. And you’re a sharp ’un, too! Well﻿—you’re young, but you’re old enough to have heard a thing or two. Did you never hear that women have got what men haven’t﻿—instinct?”

“Do you really tell me that the only reason you had for warning him against going out late at night was﻿—instinct?” asked Brereton. “Come, now!”

===Chinese===

“可这是为什么？”布里尔顿问，好奇心催着他在这出戏里也插上一脚。“您提醒他，为的是什么？”

佩特小姐转过身，细细打量这位刚发问的人。她不慌不忙、上上下下把他瞧了个透，那张古怪的脸渐渐松弛下来，浮起一丝近似微笑的神情。

“我瞧得出您是干什么的，先生，”她说。“法律界的人士！您这号人我见得多了。还是个精明人呢！好吧——您年纪轻，可也到了听过一两样事的岁数了。您就没听说过，女人有一样男人没有的东西——直觉？”

“您是要对我说，您劝他夜里别出门，唯一的缘由就是——直觉？”布里尔顿问。“得了吧！”

===Original===

“Mostly instinct, anyhow,” she answered. “Women have a sort of feeling about things that men haven’t﻿—leastways, no men that I’ve ever met had it. But of course, I’d more than that. Mr. Kitely, now, he was a townsman﻿—a London man. I’m a countrywoman. He didn’t understand﻿—you couldn’t get him to understand﻿—that it’s not safe to go walking in lonely places in country districts like this late at night. When I’d got to know his habits, I expostulated with him more than once. I pointed out to him that in spots like this, where there’s naught nearer than them houses at the foot of the hill one way, and Harborough’s cottage another way, and both of ’em a good quarter of a mile off, and where there’s all these coverts and coppices and rocks, it was not safe for an elderly man who sported a fine gold watch and chain to go wandering about in the darkness. There’s always plenty of bad characters in country places who’d knock the King himself on the head for the sake of as much as Mr. Kitely had on him, even if it was no more than the chain which every Tom and Dick could see! And it’s turned out just as I prophesied. He’s come to it!”

“But you said just now that he must have been murdered for something else than his valuables,” said Brereton.

“I said that if his papers were gone, somebody must have wanted them bad,” retorted Miss Pett. “Anyway, what’s happened is just what I felt might happen, and there he is﻿—dead. And I should be obliged to some of you if you’d send up a woman or two to help me lay him out, for I can’t be expected to do everything by myself, nor to stop in this cottage alone, neither!”

===Chinese===

“反正多半是直觉，”她答道。“女人对事情有种男人没有的感觉——起码，我见过的男人没一个有。当然喽，我有的可不止这个。就说基特利先生吧，他是个城里人——伦敦人。我是个乡下女人。他不明白——你怎么跟他说也说不明白——在这种乡野地方，深更半夜到荒僻去处走动是不安全的。等我摸清了他的习惯，就不止一回劝过他。我给他指出：在这种地方，一头是山脚下那些房子，另一头是哈伯勒的小屋，除此再没别的近邻，两头都隔着足足四分之一英里，四下尽是树丛、矮林和岩石，一个上了年纪、揣着精致金怀表金表链的人摸黑四处乱走，是不安全的。乡下地方从来不缺歹人，为了基特利先生身上那点东西，哪怕只是随便哪个张三李四都看得见的那条表链，他们连国王本人都敢敲闷棍！如今果然应了我的话。他遭上了！”

“可您方才还说了，他遇害必定另有什么缘故，不光是为了财物，”布里尔顿说。

“我说的是，要是他的纸片没了，那准是有人非要它们不可，”佩特小姐反驳道。“不管怎么说，眼下出的事，正是我觉着会出的事，他躺在那儿——死了。哪位要是肯给我派一两个女人上来，帮我给他净身穿衣，我就承情了，总不能指望我一个人样样都干，也不能叫我一个人守在这小屋里吧！”

===Original===

Leaving the doctor and a couple of policemen to arrange matters with the housekeeper, the sergeant went outside, followed by the others. He turned to Cotherstone.

“I’m going down to Harborough’s cottage, at the other end of the Shawl,” he said. “I don’t expect to learn aught much there﻿—yet﻿—but I can see if he’s at home, anyway. If any of you gentlemen like to come down﻿—”

Bent laid a hand on Cotherstone’s arm and turned him in the direction of his house.

“Brereton and I’ll go with the sergeant,” he said. “You must go home﻿—Lettie’ll be anxious about things. Go down with him, Mr. Garthwaite﻿—you’ll both hear more later.”

===Chinese===

警佐留下医生和两名警察同女管家料理事宜，自己走到屋外，其余的人跟着。他转向科瑟斯通。

“我这就去肖山另一头的哈伯勒家，”他说。“我不指望在那儿查出多少名堂——眼下还——不过不管怎么说，总能看看他在不在家。哪位先生愿意同去的——”

本特伸手搭在科瑟斯通的手臂上，把他朝他自家的方向拨了过去。

“布里尔顿和我陪警佐去，”他说。“您得回家去——莱蒂要为这些事着急的。加斯韦特先生，您陪他一道下山吧——回头有什么信儿，两位都会听到的。”

===Original===

To Brereton’s great surprise, Cotherstone made no objection to this summary dismissal. He and Garthwaite went off in one direction; the others, led by the observant policeman who had found the empty pocketbook and recognized the peculiar properties of the cord, turned away in another.

“Where’s this we’re going now?” asked Brereton as he and Bent followed their leaders through the trees and down the slopes of the Shawl.

“To John Harborough’s cottage﻿—at the other end of the hill,” answered Bent. “He’s the man they spoke of in there. He’s a queer character﻿—a professional pig-killer, who has other trades as well. He does a bit of rat-catching, and a bit of mole-catching﻿—and a good deal of poaching. In fact, he’s an odd person altogether, not only in character but in appearance. And the curious thing is that he’s got an exceedingly good-looking and accomplished daughter, a really superior girl who’s been well educated and earns her living as a governess in the town. Queer pair they make if you ever see them together!”

“Does she live with him?” asked Brereton.

===Chinese===

令布里尔顿大感意外的是，对这般干脆的打发，科瑟斯通竟没有半句异议。他和加斯韦特朝一头走了；其余的人由那个眼尖的警察领着——就是捡到那只空皮夹、认出那绳子特殊门道的那个——朝另一头去了。

“咱们这又是上哪儿去？”布里尔顿问。他和本特跟着领路的穿过树林，顺着肖山的山坡往下走。

“去约翰·哈伯勒（John Harborough）的小屋——在山的另一头，”本特答道。“就是在里头他们提起的那个人。他是个怪人——职业宰猪的，另外还兼着别的营生。他逮逮耗子，捉捉鼹鼠——还干不少偷猎的勾当。总而言之，他整个人都透着古怪，不光是脾性，长相也是如此。还有桩怪事：他有个出落得极其标致、多才多艺的女儿，是个真正出色的姑娘，受过良好教育，在镇上做家庭教师自食其力。你要是见着他们爷俩在一块儿，那才是一对奇特的搭配！”

“她跟着他住吗？”布里尔顿问。

===Original===

“Oh yes, she lives with him!” replied Bent. “And I believe that they’re very devoted to each other, though everybody marvels that such a man should have such a daughter. There’s a mystery about that man﻿—odd character that he is, he’s been well bred, and the folk hereabouts call him Gentleman Jack.”

“Won’t all this give the girl a fright?” suggested Brereton. “Wouldn’t it be better if somebody went quietly to the man’s cottage?”

But when they came to Harborough’s cottage, at the far end of the Shawl, it was all in darkness.

“Still, they aren’t gone to bed,” suddenly observed the policeman who had a faculty for seeing things. “There’s a good fire burning in the kitchen grate, and they wouldn’t leave that. Must be out, both of ’em.”

===Chinese===

“哦，跟着，她跟着他住！”本特答道。“而且我相信他们爷俩感情深着呢，虽说人人都纳闷，这样的人怎么会有这样的女儿。那个人身世里藏着谜——别看脾性古怪，他是受过良好教养的，这一带的人都管他叫‘绅士杰克’。”

“这么一来，岂不吓坏了那姑娘？”布里尔顿提议道。“要是派个人悄悄先去他家说一声，岂不更好？”

可等他们走到肖山尽头哈伯勒的小屋跟前，屋里一片漆黑。

“话说回来，他们还没睡下呢，”那个天生会瞧事的警察忽然说道。“厨房炉膛里的火烧得旺着呢，他们不会撂下火就走。准是爷俩都出去了。”

===Original===

“Go in and knock quietly,” counselled the sergeant.

He followed the policeman up the flagged walk to the cottage door, and the other two presently went after them. In the starlight Brereton looked round at these new surroundings﻿—an old, thatched cottage, set in a garden amongst trees and shrubs, with a lean-to shed at one end of it, and over everything an atmosphere of silence.

The silence was suddenly broken. A quick, light step sounded on the flagged path behind them, and the policemen turned their lamps in its direction. And Brereton, looking sharply round, became aware of the presence of a girl, who looked at these visitors wonderingly out of a pair of beautiful grey eyes.

===Chinese===

“进去，轻轻敲门，”警佐嘱咐道。

他跟着那名警察走上石板小径，到了小屋门口，另外两人随即也跟了上去。星光下，布里尔顿环顾这处新到的地方——一座古老的茅顶小屋，坐落在树木灌丛之间的园子里，屋一头搭着个单坡棚，四下里笼罩着一片静谧。

寂静蓦地被打破了。身后石板小径上响起一阵轻快的脚步声，警察们纷纷把手里的灯转了过去。布里尔顿霍然回头，发觉来了一个姑娘，正睁着一双美丽的灰眼睛，诧异地打量这群不速之客。

## VI. The Mayor / 六　市长

===Original===
Here, then, thought Brereton, was Gentleman Jack’s daughter﻿—the girl of whom Bent had just been telling him. He looked at her narrowly as she stood confronting the strange group. A self-possessed young woman, he said to himself﻿—beyond a little heightening of colour, a little questioning look about eyes and lips she showed no trace of undue surprise or fear. Decidedly a good-looking young woman, too, and not at all the sort of daughter that a man of queer character would be supposed to have﻿—refined features, an air of breeding, a suggestion of culture. And he noticed that as he and Bent raised their hats, the two policemen touched their helmets﻿—they were evidently well acquainted with the girl, and eyed her with some misgiving as well as respect.

“Beg pardon, miss,” said the sergeant, who was obviously anything but pleased with his task. “But it’s like this, d’you see?﻿—your father, now, does he happen to be at home?”

“What is it you want?” she asked. And beginning a glance of inquiry at the sergeant she finished it at Bent. “Has something happened, Mr. Bent?” she went on. “If you want my father, and he’s not in, then I don’t know where he is﻿—he went out early in the evening, and he hadn’t returned when I left the house an hour ago.”

“I daresay it’s nothing,” replied Bent. “But the fact is that something has happened. Your neighbour at the other end of the wood﻿—old Mr. Kitely, you know﻿—he’s been found dead.”

Brereton, closely watching the girl, saw that this conveyed nothing to her, beyond the mere announcement. She moved towards the door of the cottage, taking a key from her muff.
===Chinese===
这么说，眼前这位便是“绅士杰克”（Gentleman Jack）的女儿了——布里尔顿心想，本特方才向他讲起的正是这个姑娘。她站在那儿，面对着这群奇怪的来客，布里尔顿细细打量着她。一个沉得住气的年轻女子，他暗自评断——除了脸色略微泛红、眼梢嘴角带一点探询的神情之外，她没有显出半分过分的惊诧或惧怕。而且分明是个长相标致的年轻女子，全然不像人们以为一个性情古怪的男人会有的那种女儿——五官清秀，气度娴雅，透着几分教养。他还注意到，他和本特抬帽致意时，那两个警察也碰了碰头盔——他们显然同这姑娘很熟，看她的眼神里既带着几分敬重，又带着几分疑虑。

“对不起，小姐，”警佐说，他对这件差事显然怎么也高兴不起来，“事情是这样，您明白吗？——令尊呢，这会儿他碰巧在家吗？”

“你们想要什么？”她问。她那探询的一瞥先落在警佐身上，末了收回到本特身上。“出什么事了吗，本特先生？”她接着说，“你们要是找我父亲，他不在家，那我就不知道他在哪儿了——他傍晚一早就出去了，一个钟头前我离家的时候，他还没回来。”

“我但愿没什么大事，”本特答道，“可事实上是真的出了事。树林那一头您的邻居——老基特利先生，您知道的——被人发现死了。”

布里尔顿紧盯着姑娘，看出这话除了消息本身，并没有让她听出别的什么意味。她朝村舍门口走去，从暖手筒里掏出一把钥匙。

===Original===
“Yes?” she said. “And﻿—I suppose you want my father to help? He may be in﻿—he may have gone to bed.”

She unlocked the door, walked into the open living-room, and turning up a lamp which stood on the table, glanced around her.

“No,” she continued. “He’s not come in﻿—so﻿—”

“Better tell her, Mr. Bent,” whispered the sergeant. “No use keeping it back, sir﻿—she’ll have to know.”

“The fact is,” said Bent, “Mr. Kitely﻿—we’re afraid﻿—has been murdered.”
===Chinese===
“是吗？”她说，“那么——你们是要我父亲帮忙吧？他也许在家——也许已经睡下了。”

她开了锁，走进敞着门的起居室，拧亮桌上立着的灯，朝四下里扫了一眼。

“没有，”她接着说，“他没回来——那么——”

“还是直说了吧，本特先生，”警佐悄声道，“瞒不住的，先生——她反正都得知道。”

“实际情况是，”本特说，“基特利先生——我们担心——是被人谋害的。”

===Original===
The girl turned sharply at that; her eyes dilated, and a brighter tinge of colour came into her cheeks.

“Murdered!” she exclaimed. “Shot?”

Her eyes went past Bent to a corner of the room, and Brereton, following them, saw that there stood a gun, placed amongst a pile of fishing-rods and similar sporting implements. Her glance rested on it for only the fraction of a second; then it went back to Bent’s face.

“I’d better tell you everything,” said Bent quietly. “Mr. Kitely has been strangled. And the piece of cord with which it was done is﻿—so the police here say﻿—just such a piece as might have been cut off one of the cords which your father uses in his trade, you know.”
===Chinese===
姑娘闻言猛地转过身来；她双眼睁大，脸颊上泛起更浓的红晕。

“谋害！”她失声道，“枪杀的？”

她的目光越过本特，投向房间的一个角落；布里尔顿顺着看去，只见那儿立着一支猎枪，插在一堆钓竿和类似的运动器具中间。她的视线在那上头只停了一瞬，便又回到本特脸上。

“我还是把一切都告诉您吧，”本特平静地说，“基特利先生是被勒死的。而干这件事用的那截绳子——这儿的警察说——正像是从令尊营生里使的那种绳子上割下来的一截，您知道的。”

===Original===
“We aren’t suggesting aught, you know, Miss Avice,” remarked the sergeant. “Don’t go for to think that﻿—at present. But, you see, Harborough, he might have one o’ those cords hanging about somewhere, and﻿—do you understand?”

The girl had become very quiet, looking steadily from one man to the other. Once more her eyes settled on Bent.

“Do you know why Kitely was killed?” she asked suddenly. “Have you seen any reason for it?”

“He had been robbed, after his death,” answered Bent. “That seems absolutely certain.”

“Whatever you may say, you’ve got some suspicion about my father,” she remarked after a pause. “Well﻿—all I can say is, my father has no need to rob anybody﻿—far from it, if you want the truth. But what do you want?” she continued, a little impatiently. “My father isn’t in, and I don’t know where he is﻿—often he is out all night.”
===Chinese===
“我们眼下可不是疑心什么，您知道的，艾维丝（Avice）小姐，”警佐说道，“千万别往那上头想——眼下还不。可您瞧，哈伯勒（Harborough）先生那儿，兴许正巧就有那么一根绳子，随手搁在什么地方，于是——您明白吗？”

姑娘变得十分安静，目光从一个人身上沉稳地移到另一个人身上。随后她的眼睛又一次落到了本特身上。

“你们知道基特利为什么被杀吗？”她突然问，“你们看出什么缘由没有？”

“他死了之后遭了劫，”本特答道，“这一点看来是确凿无疑的。”

“随你们怎么说吧，你们总是疑心到我父亲头上了，”她顿了顿，说道，“好吧——我只能说，我父亲根本犯不着去抢任何人——您要听实话，他离那步田地远着呢。可你们到底想要什么？”她接着说，语气有些不耐烦，“我父亲不在家，我也不知道他在哪儿——他常常整夜都在外头。”

===Original===
“If we could just look round his shed, now?” said the sergeant. “Just to see if aught’s missing, like, you know. You see, miss﻿—”

“You can look round the shed﻿—and round anywhere else,” said Avice. “Though what good that will do﻿—well, you know where the shed is.”

She turned away and began taking off her hat and coat, and the four men went out into the garden and turned to the lean-to shed at the end of the cottage. A tiled verandah ran along the front of cottage and shed, and the door of the shed was at its further end. But as the sergeant was about to open it, the policeman of the observant nature made his third discovery. He had been flashing the light of his bull’s-eye lamp over his surroundings, and he now turned it on a coil of rope which hung from a nail in the boarded wall of the shed, between the door and the window.

“There you are, gentlemen!” he said, lifting the lamp in one hand and pointing triumphantly to a definite point of the coiled cord with the index finger of the other. “There! Cut clean, too﻿—just like the bit up yonder!”

Brereton pressed forward and looked narrowly at what the man was indicating. There was no doubt that a length of cord had been freshly cut off the coil, and cut, too, with an unusually sharp, keen-bladed knife; the edges of the severance were clean and distinct, the separated strands were fresh and unsoiled. It was obvious that a piece of that cord had been cut from the rest within a very short time, and the sergeant shook his head gravely as he took the coil down from its nail.
===Chinese===
“那我们能不能到他的棚屋里瞧一瞧呢？”警佐说，“就是看看有没有短了什么东西，您明白的。是这样，小姐——”

“你们可以到棚屋里看——想到哪儿看都行，”艾维丝说，“虽然那能顶什么用——也罢，你们知道棚屋在哪儿。”

她转过身去，动手摘帽子脱外衣；四个男人则出了屋，走进园子，朝村舍尽头那间单坡披屋走去。一条铺瓦的游廊沿着村舍和棚屋的正面延伸过去，棚屋的门在游廊的另一头。可警佐正要开门时，那个生性眼尖的警察作出了他的第三项发现。他一直用牛眼提灯朝四下里照来照去，这时把灯光照向了棚屋木板墙上一枚钉子挂着的一卷绳子，那位置正在门和窗之间。

“瞧这儿，先生们！”他说，一手举着提灯，另一手的食指得意地指向那卷绳子上的一处，“瞧！也是齐刷刷割断的——跟上头那截一模一样！”

布里尔顿凑上前去，细细察看那人指的地方。毫无疑问，不久之前刚有人从这卷绳子上割下了一段，而且用的是一把异常锋利、刃口极快的刀子；断口的边缘干净利落、轮廓分明，散开的绳股崭新洁净。显而易见，就在很短的时间之内，有人从这卷绳子上割走了那么一截；警佐把绳卷从钉子上取下来时，神色凝重地摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I don’t think there’s any need to look round much further, Mr. Bent,” he said. “Of course, I shall take this away with me, and compare it with the shorter piece. But we’ll just peep into this shed, so as to make his daughter believe that was what we wanted: I don’t want to frighten her more than we have done. Naught there, you see,” he went on, opening the shed door and revealing a whitewashed interior furnished with fittings and articles of its owner’s trade. “Well, we’ll away﻿—with what we’ve got.”

He went back to the door of the cottage and putting his head inside called gently to its occupant.

“Well?” demanded Avice.

“All right, miss﻿—we’re going,” said the sergeant. “But if your father comes in, just ask him to step down to the police-station, d’you see?﻿—I should like to have a word or two with him.”

The girl made no answer to this gentle request, and when the sergeant had joined the others, she shut the door of the cottage, and Brereton heard it locked and bolted.
===Chinese===
“我看没必要再四处看了，本特先生，”他说，“当然，这卷绳子我要带回去，跟那一小截比对比对。不过咱们还是到这棚屋里扫一眼，好叫他女儿相信咱们要的就是这个：我不想把她吓得比现在已经吓到的更厉害。里头没什么，您瞧，”他接着说，一面推开棚屋的门，露出刷了白灰的内部，摆着主人营生的各种设施和物件，“好了，咱们这就走——带着到手的东西。”

他走回村舍门口，把头探进去，轻声唤屋里的人。

“怎么样？”艾维丝问。

“没别的事了，小姐——我们这就走，”警佐说，“不过令尊要是回来了，麻烦您让他到警察局去一趟，您明白吗？——我想跟他说一两句话。”

对这客气的请求，姑娘没有答话。等警佐回到众人中间，她便关上了村舍的门，布里尔顿听见落锁上闩的声音。

===Original===
“That’s about the strangest thing of all!” he said as he and Bent left the policemen and turned down a by-lane which led towards the town. “I haven’t a doubt that the piece of cord with which Kitely was strangled was cut off that coil! Now what does it mean? Of course, to me it’s the very surest proof that this man Harborough had nothing to do with the murder.”

“Why?” asked Bent.

“Why? My dear fellow!” exclaimed Brereton. “Do you really think that any man who was in possession of his senses would do such a thing? Take a piece of cord from a coil﻿—leave the coil where anybody could find it﻿—strangle a man with the severed piece and leave it round the victim’s neck? Absurd! No﻿—a thousand times no!”

“Well﻿—and what then?” asked Bent.

“Ah! Somebody cut that piece off﻿—for the use it was put to,” answered Brereton. “But﻿—who?”
===Chinese===
“这可真是整件事里最古怪的一处！”他和本特辞别警察、拐上一条通往镇子的小巷时，说道，“勒死基特利的那截绳子就是从那卷绳子上割下来的，这一点我毫不怀疑！可这说明了什么？当然，在我看来，这恰恰是最确凿的证据，证明哈伯勒这个人和谋杀案毫不相干。”

“为什么？”本特问。

“为什么？亲爱的伙计！”布里尔顿叫了起来，“你真以为一个神志清醒的人会干出这种事来？从一卷绳子上割下一截——把绳卷留在谁都能发现的地方——用割下来的那截把人勒死，还把它留在死者的脖子上？荒唐！不——一千个不，一万个不！”

“那么——接下来呢？”本特问。

“啊！那截绳子是有人割下来的——就是为了派那个用场，”布里尔顿答道，“可是——是谁呢？”

===Original===
Bent made no reply for a while. Then, as they reached the outskirts of the town, he clapped a hand on his companion’s arm.

“You’re forgetting something﻿—in spite of your legal mind,” he said. “The murderer may have been interrupted before he could remove it. And in that case﻿—”

He stopped suddenly as a gate opened in the wall of a garden which they were just passing, and a tall man emerged. In the light of the adjacent lamp Bent recognized Mallalieu. Mallalieu, too, recognized him, and stopped.

“Oh, that you, Mr. Mayor!” exclaimed Bent. “I was just wondering whether to drop in on you as I passed. Have you heard what’s happened tonight?”

“Heard naught,” replied Mallalieu. “I’ve just been having a hand at whist with Councillor Northrop and his wife and daughter. What has happened, then?”
===Chinese===
本特有一会儿没有作声。待到两人走到镇子边上，他一只手拍在同伴的臂上。

“你忘了一件事——尽管你有颗法律的头脑，”他说，“凶手也许还没来得及把绳子取下来，就被人打断了。倘若是那样——”

正走过一座园子的围墙时，墙上一扇门忽然开了，一个高个子男人走了出来，他猛地收住了话头。在近旁路灯的光亮下，本特认出是马拉柳。马拉柳也认出了他，便停下了脚步。

“哟，是您哪，市长先生！”本特叫道，“我正想着路过时要不要进去看看您呢。今晚出的事，您听说了吗？”

“什么也没听说，”马拉柳答道，“我刚在镇议员诺思罗普（Northrop）家里，跟他夫妇俩和女儿打惠斯特牌。到底出了什么事？”

===Original===
They were all three walking towards the town by that time, and Bent slipped between Brereton and Mallalieu and took the Mayor’s arm.

“Murder’s happened,” he said. “That’s the plain truth of it. You know old Kitely﻿—your partner’s tenant? Well, somebody’s killed him.”

The effect of this announcement on Mallalieu was extraordinary. Bent felt the arm into which he had just slipped his own literally quiver with a spasmodic response to the astonished brain; the pipe which Mallalieu was smoking fell from his lips; out of his lips came something very like a cry of dismay.

“God bless me!” he exclaimed. “You don’t say so?”

“It’s a fact,” said Bent. He stopped and picked up the fallen pipe. “Sorry I let it out so clumsily﻿—I didn’t think it would affect you like that. But there it is﻿—Kitely’s been murdered. Strangled!”
===Chinese===
这时他们三人已一道朝镇子走去，本特挤到布里尔顿和马拉柳中间，挽住了市长的手臂。

“出了一起谋杀案，”他说，“这是明明白白的事实。老基特利您认识的——您合伙人家的房客？喏，有人把他杀了。”

这个消息在马拉柳身上引起的效果非同小可。本特感到自己刚刚挽上的那条手臂当真抖了一下，像是惊愕的脑子激起的一阵痉挛；马拉柳叼着的烟斗从嘴边掉了下去；他嘴里迸出一声近乎惊呼的叹叫。

“我的老天爷！”他叫道，“不会吧？”

“是事实，”本特说。他停下脚步，捡起掉落的烟斗，“抱歉我说得太冒失了——没想到会让您这样受震动。可事实就是如此——基特利被人谋害了。勒死的！”

===Original===
“Strangled!” echoed Mallalieu. “Dear﻿—dear﻿—dear! When was this, now?”

“Within the hour,” replied Bent. “Mr. Brereton here﻿—a friend of mine from London﻿—and I were spending the evening at your partner’s, when that neighbour of his, Garthwaite, came running in to tell Mr. Cotherstone that Kitely was lying dead on the Shawl. Of course we all went up.”

“Then﻿—you’ve seen him?” demanded Mallalieu. “There’s no doubt about it?”

“Doubt!” exclaimed Bent. “I should think there is no doubt! As determined a murder as ever I heard of. No﻿—there’s no doubt.”
===Chinese===
“勒死的！”马拉柳应声道，“哎呀——哎呀——哎呀！这是什么时候的事？”

“还不出一个钟头，”本特答道，“这位布里尔顿先生——我从伦敦来的朋友——和我正在您合伙人家做客，他的邻居加斯韦特跑进来告诉科瑟斯通先生，说基特利躺在肖山上死了。我们当然全都上去了。”

“那么——你们亲眼看见他了？”马拉柳追问，“这没有疑问吧？”

“疑问！”本特叫道，“我看根本谈不到疑问！我平生听说的谋杀案里，再没有比这更蓄意分明的了。不——毫无疑问。”

===Original===
Mallalieu paused﻿—at the gate of his own house.

“Come in, gentlemen,” he said. “Come in just a minute, anyway. I﻿—egad it’s struck me all of a heap, has that news! Murder?﻿—there hasn’t been such a thing in these parts ever since I came here, near thirty years ago. Come in and tell me a bit more about it.”

He led the way up a gravelled drive, admitted himself and his visitors to the house with a latchkey, and turned into a parlour where a fire burned and a small supper-tray was set out on a table beneath a lamp.

“All my folks’ll have gone to bed,” he said. “They go and leave me a bite of something, you see﻿—I’m often out late. Will you gentlemen have a sandwich﻿—or a dry biscuit? Well, you’ll have a drink, then. And so,” he went on, as he produced glasses from the sideboard, “and so you were spending the evening with Cotherstone, what?”

“Well, I can’t say that we exactly spent all the evening with him,” answered Bent, “because he had to go out for a good part of it, on business. But we were with him﻿—we were at his house﻿—when the news came.”
===Chinese===
马拉柳停住了——正停在他自家宅子的院门口。

“进来坐坐，二位先生，”他说，“哪怕只进来一分钟呢。我——嗬，这消息可把我震得不轻！谋杀？——打我到这地方来，快三十年了，这一带就没出过这种事。进来，再跟我细细讲讲。”

他在前头引路，走上一条铺着砂砾的车道，用弹簧钥匙开了门，让自己和客人进屋，转进一间客厅——炉火烧得正旺，灯下一张桌上摆好了小小的晚餐托盘。

“家里人都该睡下了，”他说，“他们总给我留点吃的，您瞧——我常常在外面待到很晚。二位先生要不要来块三明治——或者来块干饼干？好吧，那总得喝上一杯。这么说，”他从餐具柜里取出酒杯，接着说道，“这么说，你们这个晚上是在科瑟斯通那儿过的，嗯？”

“呃，倒也不能说我们一整晚都陪着他，”本特答道，“因为他有大半个晚上都得出去办事。可消息传来的时候，我们正跟他在一起——正在他家里。”

===Original===
“Aye, he had to go out, had he?” asked Mallalieu, as if from mere curiosity. “What time would that be, like? I knew he’d business tonight﻿—business of ours.”

“Nine to ten, roughly speaking,” replied Bent. “He’d just got in when Garthwaite came with the news.”

“It ’ud shock him, of course,” suggested Mallalieu. “His own tenant!”

“Yes﻿—it was a shock,” agreed Bent. He took the glass which his host handed to him and sat down. “We’d better tell you all about it,” he said. “It’s a queer affair﻿—Mr. Brereton here, who’s a barrister, thinks it’s a very queer affair.”

Mallalieu nodded and sat down, too, glass in hand. He listened attentively﻿—and Brereton watched him while he listened. A sleek, sly, observant, watchful man, this, said Brereton to himself﻿—the sort that would take all in and give little out. And he waited expectantly to hear what Mallalieu would say when he had heard everything.
===Chinese===
“哦，他得出去办事，是吗？”马拉柳问道，仿佛只是出于随便的好奇，“那大概是什么时候的事？我知道他今晚有事要办——咱们两人的事。”

“九点到十点之间，大致是这么个时候，”本特答道，“加斯韦特带着消息来的时候，他刚进门。”

“那自然要吓他一跳，”马拉柳提了一句，“他自己的房客嘛！”

“是的——确实吓了一跳，”本特表示同意。他接过主人递来的酒杯，坐了下来。“我们最好原原本本讲给您听，”他说，“这是桩蹊跷事——这位布里尔顿先生是位出庭律师，他也觉得这事蹊跷得很。”

马拉柳点点头，也拿着酒杯坐下了。他听得很专注——布里尔顿则趁他听着的时候端详他。这是个圆滑、狡黠、处处留意、时时提防的人，布里尔顿暗自思量——是那种全盘收进、绝少吐露的角色。他便等着看马拉柳听完全部之后会说些什么。

===Original===
Mallalieu turned to him when Bent had finished.

“I agree with you, sir,” he said. “Nobody but a fool would have cut that piece of cord off, left it round the man’s neck, and left the coil hanging where anybody could find it. And that man Harborough’s no fool! This isn’t his job, Bent. No!”

“Whose, then?” asked Bent.
===Chinese===
本特讲完，马拉柳转向布里尔顿。

“我同意您的看法，先生，”他说，“除非是傻子，才会割下那截绳子，把它留在死者的脖子上，还把绳卷挂在谁都能找得到的地方。而哈伯勒那个人可不是傻子！这不是他干的，本特。不是！”

“那么是谁干的？”本特问。

===Original===
Mallalieu suddenly drank off the contents of his glass and rose.

“As I’m chief magistrate, I’d better go down to see the police,” he said. “There’s been a queer character or two hanging about the town of late. I’d better stir ’em up. You won’t come down, I suppose?” he continued when they left the house together.

“No﻿—we can do no good,” answered Bent.

His own house was just across the road from Mallalieu’s, and he and Brereton said goodnight and turned towards it as the Mayor strode quickly off in the direction of the police-station.
===Chinese===
马拉柳突然一仰脖喝干了杯中酒，站起身来。

“我身为首席治安官，最好还是下去看看警察，”他说，“近来镇上晃荡着一两个形迹可疑的怪家伙。我该去催催他们。你们不一块儿下去吧，我想？”一同出了宅子之后，他又接着说。

“不去了——我们去也帮不上忙，”本特答道。

他自己的家就在马路对面，正对着马拉柳家；市长迈开大步朝警察局方向去了，他和布里尔顿道过晚安，便转身朝自家走去。

## VII. Night Work / 七　夜工

===Original===

From the little colony of new houses at the foot of the Shawl to the police station at the end of the High Street was only a few minutes’ walk. Mallalieu was a quick walker, and he covered this distance at his top speed. But during those few minutes he came to a conclusion, for he was as quick of thought as in the use of his feet.

Of course, Cotherstone had killed Kitely. That was certain. He had begun to suspect that as soon as he heard of the murder; he became convinced of it as soon as young Bent mentioned that Cotherstone had left his guests for an hour after supper. Without a doubt Cotherstone had lost his head and done this foolish thing! And now Cotherstone must be protected, safeguarded; heaven and earth must be moved lest suspicion should fall on him. For nothing could be done to Cotherstone without effect upon himself﻿—and of himself﻿—and of himself Mallalieu meant to take very good care. Never mind what innocent person suffered, Cotherstone must go free.

And the first thing to do was to assume direction of the police, to pull strings, to engineer matters. No matter how much he believed in Harborough’s innocence, Harborough was the man to go for﻿—at present. Attention must be concentrated on him, and on him only. Anything﻿—anything, at whatever cost of morals and honesty to divert suspicion from that fool of a Cotherstone!﻿—if it were not already too late. It was the desire to make sure that it was not too late, the desire to be beforehand, that made Mallalieu hasten to the police. He knew his own power, he had a supreme confidence in his ability to manage things, and he was determined to give up the night to the scheme already seething in his fertile brain rather than that justice should enter upon what he would consider a wrong course.

===Chinese===

从肖山脚下那一小片新房子到大街尽头的警察局，本来不过几分钟的脚程。马拉柳（Mallalieu）是个走路飞快的人，这一程更是拿出了顶快的步子。而就在这几分钟里，他已经把事情想出了个结论——他脑子动起来，跟两条腿一样快。

当然，是科瑟斯通（Cotherstone）杀了基特利（Kitely）。这是确定无疑的。一听说出了谋杀案，他就起了疑；等小本特（Bent）一提到科瑟斯通晚饭后把客人撂下、走开了一个钟头，他立刻就认定了。毫无疑问，是科瑟斯通一时慌了神，才干下这桩蠢事！如今必须保护科瑟斯通、保全科瑟斯通；就是翻天覆地，也不能让嫌疑落到他头上。因为对科瑟斯通无论怎样处置，都免不了牵动他自己——至于他自己嘛——他马拉柳是打定主意要把自己照料得妥妥帖帖的。管他哪个无辜的人遭殃，科瑟斯通必须安然无恙。

而头一件要办的事，就是把警察的指挥抓到自己手里，牵线布局，暗中操盘。无论他心里把哈伯勒（Harborough）的无辜信到几分，眼下要追拿的，还得是哈伯勒。注意力必须集中到他身上，而且只准集中到他一个人身上。什么都行——什么都行，不管赔上多少道义和诚实的代价，只要能把嫌疑从科瑟斯通那个蠢货身上引开！——只要还不是太迟的话。正是要弄个明白是否还不太迟、要抢在头里的那股念头，催着马拉柳直奔警察局。他清楚自己的能耐，对自己摆布事情的本事有着十足的自信；他打定主意，宁可把一整夜搭在这颗精明脑袋里早已翻腾开的计谋上，也决不容司法走上一条在他看来是错路的道儿。

===Original===

While he sat silently and intently listening to Bent’s story of the crime, Mallalieu, who could think and listen and give full attention to both mental processes without letting either suffer at the expense of the other, had reconstructed the murder. He knew Cotherstone﻿—nobody knew him half as well. Cotherstone was what Mallalieu called deep﻿—he was ingenious, resourceful, inventive. Cotherstone, in the early hours of the evening, had doubtless thought the whole thing out. He would be well acquainted with his prospective victim’s habits. He would know exactly when and where to waylay Kitely. The filching of the piece of cord from the wall of Harborough’s shed was a clever thing﻿—infernally clever, thought Mallalieu, who had a designing man’s wholehearted admiration for any sort of cleverness in his own particular line. It would be an easy thing to do﻿—and what a splendidly important thing! Of course Cotherstone knew all about Harborough’s arrangements﻿—he would often pass the pig-killer’s house﻿—from the hedge of the garden he would have seen the coils of greased rope hanging from their nails under the verandah roof, aye, a thousand times. Nothing easier than to slip into Harborough’s garden from the adjacent wood, cut off a length of the cord, use it﻿—and leave it as a first bit of evidence against a man whose public record was uncertain. Oh, very clever indeed!﻿—if only Cotherstone could carry things off, and not allow his conscience to write marks on his face. And he must help﻿—and innocent as he felt Harborough to be, he must set things going against Harborough﻿—his life was as naught, against the Mallalieu-Cotherstone safety.

Mallalieu walked into the police-station, to find the sergeant just returned and in consultation with the superintendent, whom he had summoned to hear his report. Both turned inquiringly on the Mayor.

“I’ve heard all about it,” said Mallalieu, bustling forward. “Mr. Bent told me. Now then, where’s that cord they talk about?”

===Chinese===

马拉柳坐在那儿一言不发、凝神细听本特讲述案情的时候，早已把这桩凶杀在心里从头重演了一遍——他这个人，一边听一边想，两头各用全副心思，互不耽误。他了解科瑟斯通——天底下再没有第二个人及他一半那样了解。科瑟斯通是马拉柳所说的那种“有城府”的人——机巧，多谋，点子层出不穷。当天傍晚早些时候，科瑟斯通必定已经把全盘想透了。他对那即将到手的目标的起居习惯，必定摸得一清二楚；他必能掐得准在何时何地截住基特利。从哈伯勒棚屋墙上截走那截绳索，是一步妙棋——妙得邪门，马拉柳心想；这种工于心计的人，对自己本行里的种种巧计，向来是心悦诚服地佩服的。这事儿办起来容易——而且是多么要紧、多么漂亮的一手！科瑟斯通当然对哈伯勒家的安排了如指掌——他常打那杀猪匠门前过——隔着园篱，游廊顶下挂在钉子上的一圈圈油绳，他准瞧见过，是啊，见过一千回了。从旁边的林子溜进哈伯勒的园子，割下一段绳，用掉——再留下，当作头一件物证，栽给一个来历名声不清不楚的人——再没有比这更省事的了。哦，实在是高明！——但愿科瑟斯通能把这一切遮掩过去，别让良心把印记写到脸上来。而他必须帮衬——尽管他认定哈伯勒无辜，他也必须把事情往哈伯勒身上引——比起马拉柳-科瑟斯通两家的安稳来，哈伯勒那条命，一文不值。

马拉柳走进警察局，只见警佐（sergeant）刚回来，正跟警司（superintendent）商议着——是他把警司请来听汇报的。两人都转过脸来，探询地望着市长。

“事情我都听说了，”马拉柳说着，风风火火地抢上前来。“是本特先生告诉我的。好了，他们说的那根绳在哪儿？”

===Original===

The sergeant pointed to the coil and the severed piece, which lay on a large sheet of brown paper on a side-table, preparatory to being sealed up. Mallalieu crossed over and made a short examination of these exhibits; then he turned to the superintendent with an air of decision.

“Aught been done?” he demanded.

“Not yet, Mr. Mayor,” answered the superintendent. “We were just consulting as to what’s best to be done.”

“I should think that’s obvious,” replied Mallalieu. “You must get to work! Two things you want to do just now. Ring up Norcaster for one thing, and High Gill Junction for another. Give ’em a description of Harborough﻿—he’ll probably have made for one place or another, to get away by train. And ask ’em at Norcaster to lend you a few plainclothes men, and to send ’em along here at once by motor﻿—there’s no train till morning. Then, get all your own men out﻿—now!﻿—and keep folk off the paths in that wood, and put a watch on Harborough’s house, in case he should put a bold face on it and come back﻿—he’s impudence enough﻿—and of course, if he comes, they’ll take him. Get to all that now﻿—at once!”

===Chinese===

警佐指了指那盘绳子和割下来的那一截——两样都摊在一张边桌的大幅牛皮纸上，正待封存。马拉柳走过去，把这两件物证草草查验了一番，随即带着一副决断的神气转向警司。

“采取了什么行动没有？”他催问。

“还没有，市长先生，”警司答道。“我们正商量着怎么办最妥当。”

“我看这还用说么，”马拉柳答道，“你们得赶紧干起来！眼下有两件事要办。一是给诺卡斯特（Norcaster）挂电话，二是给海吉尔枢纽站（High Gill Junction）挂。把哈伯勒的相貌特征报给他们——他多半奔了这两处中的一处，好搭火车跑掉。再叫诺卡斯特那边借你们几个便衣，立刻用汽车送过来——明早才有火车。然后，把你手底下的人全撒出去——这就去！——不许闲人踏上那林子里的各条小径，再派人在哈伯勒家盯守，防着他索性厚起脸皮杀回来——他那股横劲儿是有的——当然，他一露面，就逮他。这些现在就办——立刻办！”

===Original===

“You think it’s Harborough, then?” said the superintendent.

“I think there’s what the law folks call a prymer facy case against him,” replied Mallalieu. “It’s your duty to get him, anyway, and if he can clear himself, why, let him. Get busy with that telephone, and be particular about help from Norcaster﻿—we’re understaffed here as it is.”

The superintendent hurried out of his office and Mallalieu turned to the sergeant.

“I understood from Mr. Bent,” he said, “that that housekeeper of Kitely’s said the old fellow had been to the bank at noon today, to draw some money? That so?”

===Chinese===

“那么说，您认为是哈伯勒干的？”警司说。

“我看，这案子落在哈伯勒身上，照法律行家的说法，已经是‘表面上成立’（prymer facy）的了，”马拉柳答道。“反正，缉拿他是你们的职责；他要是能把自己洗刷清楚，那也由他去。快去忙电话吧，诺卡斯特的支援务必上心——咱们这儿人手本来就紧。”

警司急匆匆出了办公室，马拉柳便转向警佐。

“我从本特先生那儿听说，”他说道，“基特利那位管家讲，老头儿今天中午上银行取钱去了？有这么回事？”

===Original===

“So she said, your Worship,” answered the sergeant. “Some allowance, or something of that sort, that he drew once a quarter. She didn’t know how much.”

“But she thought he’d have it on him when he was attacked?” asked Mallalieu.

“She said he was a man for carrying his money on him always,” replied the sergeant. “We understood from her it was his habit. She says he always had a good bit on him﻿—as a rule. And of course, if he’d drawn more today, why, he might have a fair lot.”

“We’ll soon find that out,” remarked Mallalieu. “I’ll step round to the bank manager and rouse him. Now you get your men together﻿—this is no time for sleeping. You ought to have men up at the Shawl now.”

===Chinese===

“她是这么说的，阁下，”警佐答道。“一笔津贴还是类似的什么进项，他一个季度领一回。她不知道有多少。”

“可她认为，他遇害的时候身上带着这笔钱？”马拉柳问。

“她说他这个人，历来总是把钱贴身带着，”警佐答道。“听她的口气，这是他的老习惯。她说他身上向来揣着好一笔——照例是这样。当然，他今天要是又多取了些，嘿，那数目就可观了。”

“这个我们很快就能查个水落石出，”马拉柳说道。“我这就绕到银行经理那儿，把他从床上叫起来。你呢，去把你的人集合起来——现在可不是睡觉的时候。这会儿你就该派人守上肖山了。”

===Original===

“I’ve left one man at Kitely’s cottage, sir, and another about Harborough’s﻿—in case Harborough should come back during the night,” said the sergeant. “We’ve two more constables close by the station. I’ll get them up.”

“Do it just now,” commanded Mallalieu. “I’ll be back in a while.”

He hurried out again and went rapidly down the High Street to the old-fashioned building near the Town Hall in which the one bank of the little town did its business, and in which the bank manager lived. There was not a soul about in the street, and the ringing of the bell at the bank-house door, and the loud knock which Mallalieu gave in supplement to it, seemed to wake innumerable echoes. And proof as he believed himself to be against such slight things, the sudden opening of a window above his head made him jump.

The startled bank-manager, hurrying down to his midnight visitor in his dressing-gown and slippers, stood aghast when he had taken the Mayor within and learned his errand.

===Chinese===

“我已经留了一个人在基特利的村舍，先生，还有一个人在哈伯勒家左近——防着哈伯勒夜里回来，”警佐说。“局子近旁我们还有两个警察。我把他们叫起来。”

“这就去办，”马拉柳命令道。“我过一会儿就回来。”

他又匆匆出去，顺着大街疾步而下，直奔市政厅附近那座老式房子——小镇上唯一的一家银行在那儿营业，银行经理也住在里头。街上空无一人，银行宅门上的铃响，加上马拉柳补上的几记重重的敲门，仿佛惊醒了无数回声。他自认是不为这点区区小事所动的人，可头顶上一扇窗子猛然打开，还是叫他吓了一跳。

受了惊的银行经理穿着晨衣、趿着拖鞋，匆匆下楼来见这位深夜来客；把市长让进屋、听明来意之后，他惊得呆在了那里。

===Original===

“Certainly!” he said. “Kitely was in the bank today, about noon﻿—I attended to him myself. That’s the second time he’s been here since he came to the town. He called here a day or two after he first took that house from Mr. Cotherstone﻿—to cash a draft for his quarter’s pension. He told me then who he was. Do you know?”

“Not in the least,” replied Mallalieu, telling the lie all the more readily because he had been fully prepared for the question to which it was an answer. “I knew naught about him.”

“He was an ex-detective,” said the bank-manager. “Pensioned off, of course: a nice pension. He told me he’d had﻿—I believe it was getting on to forty years’ service in the police force. Dear, dear, this is a sad business﻿—and I’m afraid I can tell you a bit more about it.”

===Chinese===

“可不是嘛！”他说。“基特利今天来过银行，中午前后——是我亲自接待的。这是他来镇上以后第二回了。他头一回从科瑟斯通先生手里租下那房子之后一两天，来兑过一张季度养老金汇票——他的身份就是在那时候告诉我的。您可知道？”

“一点儿也不知道，”马拉柳答道。这个问题他早有准备，答话的腹稿也早打好了，因此这谎撒得格外顺溜。“我对他原先是一无所知。”

“他是个退职侦探，”银行经理说。“当然，是领了退休金退下来的：数目还颇可观。他告诉我他干过——我记得是快满四十年的警界差事。哎呀呀，这真是件惨事——而且，恐怕我还能再告诉您一点情况。”

===Original===

“What?” demanded Mallalieu, showing surprise in spite of himself.

“You mentioned Harborough,” said the bank-manager, shaking his head.

“Well?” said Mallalieu. “What then?”

“Harborough was at the counter when Kitely took his money,” answered the bank-manager. “He had called in to change a five-pound note.”

The two men looked at each other in silence for a time. Then the bank-manager shook his head again.

===Chinese===

“什么？”马拉柳追问，不由自主地露出讶异之色。

“您方才提到了哈伯勒，”银行经理说着，摇了摇头。

“嗯？”马拉柳说。“怎么样？”

“基特利取钱的时候，哈伯勒就在柜台跟前，”银行经理答道。“他是顺路进来兑一张五镑钞票的。”

两人默默对视了一阵。然后银行经理又摇了摇头。

===Original===

“You wouldn’t think that a man who has a five-pound note of his own to change would be likely, to murder another man for what he could get,” he went on. “But Kitely had a nice bit of money to carry away, and he wore a very valuable gold watch and chain, which he was rather fond of showing in the town, and﻿—eh?”

“It’s a suspicious business,” said Mallalieu. “You say Harborough saw Kitely take his money?”

“Couldn’t fail,” replied the bank-manager. “He was standing by him. The old man put it﻿—notes and gold﻿—in a pocket that he had inside his waistcoat.”

===Chinese===

“您想啊，一个自己有五镑钞票要兑的人，照理不至于为着劫财去谋害旁人，”他接着说。“可基特利那天带走的是好一笔钱，还戴着一副贵重的金表和表链——他在镇上就好向人夸示这个，还有——呃？”

“这案子疑点重重，”马拉柳说。“你是说，哈伯勒亲眼看见基特利取钱了？”

“不可能看不见，”银行经理答道。“他就站在老头儿身边。老头儿把钱——钞票和金币——放进了马甲里头贴身的一个口袋。”

===Original===

Mallalieu lingered, as if in thought, rubbing his chin and staring at the carpet. “Well, that’s a sort of additional clue,” he remarked at last. “It looks very black against Harborough.”

“We’ve the numbers of the notes that I handed to Kitely,” observed the bank-manager. “They may be useful if there’s any attempt to change any note, you know.”

Mallalieu shook his head.

“Aye, just so,” he answered. “But I should say there won’t be﻿—just yet. It’s a queer business, isn’t it﻿—but, as I say, there’s evidence against this fellow, and we must try to get him.”

===Chinese===

马拉柳磨蹭着不走，像是在思量，一面摩挲下巴，一面盯着地毯瞧。“唔，这算得上一条补充线索，”他终于开口道。“对哈伯勒可是大大的不利。”

“我付给基特利那些钞票的编号，我们都记着呢，”银行经理说道。“您知道，万一有人拿钞票出去兑，也许用得上。”

马拉柳摇了摇头。

“是啊，话是这么说，”他答道，“可依我看，一时半会儿还不会有人去兑。这事透着古怪，是不是——不过，正如我方才说的，这小子身上有证据可指，我们必须设法把他拿住。”

===Original===

He went out then and crossed the street to the doctor’s house﻿—while he was about it, he wanted to know all he could. And with the doctor he stopped much longer than he had stopped at the bank, and when he left him he was puzzled. For the doctor said to him what he had said to Cotherstone and to Bent and to the rest of the group in the wood﻿—that whoever had strangled Kitely had had experience in that sort of grim work before﻿—or else he was a sailorman who had expert knowledge of tying knots. Now Mallalieu was by that time more certain than ever that Cotherstone was the murderer, and he felt sure that Cotherstone had no experience of that sort of thing.

“Done with a single twist and a turn!” he muttered to himself as he walked back to the police-station. “Aye﻿—aye!﻿—that seems to show knowledge. But it’s not my business to follow that up just now﻿—I know what my business is﻿—nobody better.”

The superintendent and the sergeant were giving orders to two sleepy-eyed policemen when Mallalieu rejoined them. He waited until the policemen had gone away to patrol the Shawl and then took the superintendent aside.

===Chinese===

随后他出门穿过街道，到医生家去了——既然人已经出来了，能打听的他就要打听个遍。在医生那里，他耽搁的工夫比在银行长得多；离开的时候，心里却犯起了嘀咕。因为医生对他说的，跟对科瑟斯通、本特和林子里那一伙人说的一模一样——勒死基特利的人，先前干过这类阴森营生——再不然，就是个精于打绳结的行家水手。可这时的马拉柳，比先前任何时候都更确信凶手就是科瑟斯通，而他也很有把握：这类勾当，科瑟斯通是个生手。

“一拧一转就完事！”他一面往警察局走，一面自言自语。“是啊——是啊！——这看来是懂行的门道。可眼下追这条线不是我的正事——我的正事是什么，我清楚——没人比我更清楚。”

马拉柳回到警察局时，警司和警佐正对着两个睡眼惺忪的警察交代差事。他等那两个警察出发上肖山巡逻去了，才把警司拉到一边。

===Original===

“I’ve heard a bit more incriminatory news against Harborough,” he said. “He was in the bank this morning﻿—or yesterday morning, as it now is﻿—when Kitely drew his money. There may be naught in that﻿—and there may be a lot. Anyway, he knew the old man had a goodish bit on him.”

The superintendent nodded, but his manner was doubtful.

“Well, of course, that’s evidence﻿—considering things,” he said, “but you know as well as I do, Mr. Mayor, that Harborough’s not a man that’s ever been in want of money. It’s the belief of a good many folks in the town that he has money of his own: he’s always been a bit of a mystery ever since I can remember. He could afford to give that daughter of his a good education﻿—good as a young lady gets﻿—and he spends plenty, and I never heard of him owing aught. Of course, he’s a queer lot﻿—we know he’s a poacher and all that, but he’s so skilful about it that we’ve never been able to catch him. I can’t think he’s the guilty party﻿—and yet﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我又听到一条对哈伯勒罪嫌更重的消息，”他说。“今天早上——如今该说昨天早上了——基特利取钱的时候，他就在银行里。这里头兴许什么都没有——也兴许名堂大得很。反正，他知道那老头儿身上揣着不小的一笔。”

警司点了点头，神色却将信将疑。

“唔，这当然算证据——通盘看下来的话，”他说，“可是市长先生，您跟我一样清楚，哈伯勒从来不是个缺钱花的人。镇上有好些人一直相信他自有积蓄：打我记事起，他这人就一直有几分神秘。他供得起他那女儿受好教育——好到年轻小姐所能受的顶好的那种——他花钱也大方，我从没听说他欠过谁一文。当然，他是个古怪人物——我们知道他偷猎什么的，可他手段太高明，我们从来没能抓住他。我想不出他会是凶手——可是——”

===Original===

“You can’t get away from the facts,” said Mallalieu. “He’ll have to be sought for. If he’s made himself scarce﻿—if he doesn’t come home﻿—”

“Ah, that ’ud certainly be against him!” agreed the superintendent. “Well, I’m doing all I can. We’ve got our own men out, and there’s three officers coming over from Norcaster by motor﻿—they’re on the way now.”

“Send for me if aught turns up,” said Mallalieu.

He walked slowly home, his brain still busy with possibilities and eventualities. And within five minutes of his waking at his usual hour of six it was again busy﻿—and curious. For he and Cotherstone, both keen business men who believed in constant supervision of their workmen, were accustomed to meet at the yard at half-past six every morning, summer or winter, and he was wondering what his partner would say and do﻿—and look like.

===Chinese===

“事实摆在那儿，绕是绕不开的，”马拉柳说。“总得去寻他。他要是躲了起来——要是不回家——”

“啊，那样的话，对他可就大大不利了！”警司深以为然。“好吧，我正尽力办着。我们自己的人已经撒出去了，还有三名警官正从诺卡斯特坐汽车赶来——这会儿已经在路上。”

“有什么动静，就打发人来叫我，”马拉柳说。

他缓步走回家去，脑子里还在盘算种种可能与变数。到了惯常六点钟醒来的时候，不出五分钟，那脑子又转起来了——还带着几分好奇。因为他和科瑟斯通这两个精明的生意人，都信奉时时刻刻督看工人那一套，无论寒暑，每天早晨六点半总要在院子里碰头；他正琢磨着，他的合伙人会说什么、会做什么——又会是一副什么神情。

===Original===

Cotherstone was in the yard when Mallalieu reached it. He was giving some orders to a carter, and he finished what he was doing before coming up to Mallalieu. In the half light of the morning he looked pretty much as usual﻿—but Mallalieu noticed a certain worn look under his eyes and suppressed nervousness in his voice. He himself remained silent and observant, and he let Cotherstone speak first.

“Well?” said Cotherstone, coming close to him as they stood in a vacant space outside the office. “Well?”

“Well?” responded Mallalieu.

===Chinese===

马拉柳到院子时，科瑟斯通已经在那儿了。他正吩咐一个车夫什么话，把手头的事了结了，才踱到马拉柳跟前来。在清晨的微光里，他瞧上去同平常差不多——可马拉柳注意到，他眼睛底下带着一缕倦色，嗓音里压着一股紧张的劲儿。他自己一言不发，只冷眼观察，由着科瑟斯通先开口。

“怎么样？”科瑟斯通说着，凑近他身边；两人正站在办公室外一片空场上。“怎么样？”

“怎么样？”马拉柳应道。

===Original===

Cotherstone began to fidget with some account books and papers that he had brought from his house. He eyed his partner with furtive glances; Mallalieu eyed him with steady and watchful ones.

“I suppose you’ve heard all about it?” said Cotherstone, after an awkward silence.

“Aye!” replied Mallalieu, drily. “Aye, I’ve heard.”

Cotherstone looked round. There was no one near him, but he dropped his voice to a whisper.

“So long as nobody but him knew,” he muttered, giving Mallalieu another side glance, “so long as he hadn’t said aught to anybody﻿—and I don’t think he had﻿—we’re﻿—safe.”

===Chinese===

科瑟斯通摆弄起他从家里带来的几本账册和文件，手上不安生。他瞟合伙人，眼神躲躲闪闪；马拉柳瞧他，目光沉稳而带提防。

“想必你全听说了？”僵了半晌，科瑟斯通开口道。

“是啊！”马拉柳干巴巴地答道。“是啊，听说了。”

科瑟斯通四下里张望。身边没有人，他还是把嗓音压成了耳语。

“只要除了他再没旁人知道，”他咕哝道，又斜眼瞟了瞟马拉柳，“只要他不曾对任何人吐露半个字——我看他是没有——咱们就——安全了。”

===Original===

Mallalieu was still staring quietly at Cotherstone. And Cotherstone began to grow restless under that steady, questioning look.

“Oh?” observed Mallalieu, at last. “Aye? You think so? Ah!”

“Good God﻿—don’t you!” exclaimed Cotherstone, roused to a sudden anger. “Why﻿—”

But just then a policeman came out of the High Street into the yard, caught sight of the two partners, and came over to them, touching his helmet.

“Can your Worship step across the way?” he asked. “They’ve brought Harborough down, and the Super wants a word with you.”

===Chinese===

马拉柳依旧静静地盯着科瑟斯通瞧。在那道沉稳的、探问的目光底下，科瑟斯通渐渐不安起来。

“哦？”马拉柳终于开腔。“是吗？你这么想？啊！”

“老天爷——难道你不这么想！”科瑟斯通勃然动了火，嚷了起来。“这——”

可正在这时，一个警察从大街拐进院子，一眼望见这两位合伙人，便朝他们走过来，抬手碰了碰头盔。

“阁下能劳驾过街走一趟吗？”他问。“他们把哈伯勒押下来了，警司有话要跟您讲。”

## VIII. Retained for the Defence / 八　受聘辩护

===Original===

Instead of replying to the policeman by word or movement, Mallalieu glanced at Cotherstone. There was a curious suggestion in that glance which Cotherstone did not like. He was already angry; Mallalieu’s inquiring look made him still angrier.

“Like to come?” asked Mallalieu, laconically.

“No!” answered Cotherstone, turning towards the office. “It’s naught to me.”

===Chinese===

马拉柳没有用言语或动作回应那名警察，只是朝科瑟斯通瞥了一眼。那一瞥里含着一种说不清的意味，叫科瑟斯通很不受用。他本来就在气头上；马拉柳这探询的一眼更是火上浇油。

“想去吗？”马拉柳言简意赅地问。

“不去！”科瑟斯通答道，转身朝账房走去。“这事与我无关。”

===Original===

He disappeared within doors, and Mallalieu walked out of the yard into the High Street﻿—to run against Bent and Brereton, who were hurrying in the direction of the police-station, in company with another constable.

“Ah!” said Mallalieu as they met. “So you’ve heard, too, I suppose? Heard that Harborough’s been taken, I mean. Now, how was he taken?” he went on, turning to the policeman who had summoned him. “And when, and where?﻿—let’s be knowing about it.”

===Chinese===

他转身进了屋；马拉柳则走出院落，来到主街上——迎面正撞上本特和布里尔顿，两人正由另一名警察陪着，急匆匆朝警察局方向赶去。

“啊！”见面时马拉柳说道。“这么说，你们也听说了？我是说，听说哈伯勒被拿下了。那么，他是怎么被拿下的？”他转向来传他的那名警察，接着问道。“什么时候，在哪儿？——咱们得弄个明白。”

===Original===

“He wasn’t taken, your Worship,” replied the man. “Leastways, not in what you’d call the proper way. He came back to his house half an hour or so ago﻿—when it was just getting nicely light﻿—and two of our men that were there told him what was going on, and he appeared to come straight down with them. He says he knows naught, your Worship.”

“That’s what you’d expect,” remarked Mallalieu, drily. “He’d be a fool if he said aught else.”

He put his thumbs in the armholes of his waistcoat, and, followed by the others, strolled into the police-station as if he were dropping in on business of trifling importance. And there was nothing to be seen there which betokened that a drama of life and death was being constructed in that formal-looking place of neutral-coloured walls, precise furniture, and atmosphere of repression. Three or four men stood near the superintendent’s desk; a policeman was writing slowly and laboriously on a big sheet of blue paper at a side-table, a woman was coaxing a sluggish fire to burn.

===Chinese===

“他不是被拿下的，阁下，”那人答道。“至少，不是您说的那种正经路数拿下的。约莫半个钟头前，天刚亮利落的时候，他回了家——我们守在那儿的两个人把事情跟他说了，他看样子就自己跟着他们直接来了。他说他什么都不知道，阁下。”

“那还用说，”马拉柳干巴巴地说。“他要是说出别的什么来，那才是傻瓜呢。”

他把两根拇指插进背心的袖孔里，由众人跟着，踱进了警察局，仿佛只是顺路进来办件无关紧要的小事。那是一间样子一本正经的屋子：四壁是不深不浅的颜色，家具规规矩矩，透着一股压人的气氛——任什么也瞧不出，一场关乎生死的戏正从旁拼凑成形。警司的写字台边站着三四个人；靠墙的一张桌子上，一名警察正伏在一张蓝色大纸上，慢慢腾腾、一笔一画地写着；一个女人正哄着一炉懒洋洋不肯烧旺的火。

===Original===

“The whole thing’s ridiculous!” said a man’s scornful voice. “It shouldn’t take five seconds to see that.”

Brereton instinctively picked out the speaker. That was Harborough, of course﻿—the tall man who stood facing the others and looking at them as if he wondered how they could be as foolish as he evidently considered them to be. He looked at this man with great curiosity. There was certainly something noticeable about him, he decided. A wiry, alert, keen-eyed man, with good, somewhat gipsy-like features, much tanned by the weather, as if he were perpetually exposed to sun and wind, rain and hail; sharp of movement, evidently of more than ordinary intelligence, and, in spite of his rough garments and fur cap, having an indefinable air of gentility and breeding about him. Brereton had already noticed the pitch and inflection of his voice; now, as Harborough touched his cap to the Mayor, he noticed that his hands, though coarsened and weather-browned, were well-shaped and delicate. Something about him, something in his attitude, the glance of his eye, seemed to indicate that he was the social superior of the policemen, uniformed or plain-clothed, who were watching him with speculative and slightly puzzled looks.

===Chinese===

“这整件事荒唐透顶！”一个男人轻蔑的声音说道。“要看出这一点，本该用不着五秒钟。”

布里尔顿凭本能一眼认出了说话的人。那自然就是哈伯勒——那个高个子男人面对众人站着，瞧他们的神情，仿佛在纳闷这些人怎么能愚蠢到他那心目中的地步。他怀着极大的好奇心打量此人。此人身上确实有样惹眼的东西，他心里断定。那是个精瘦干练、目光锐利的汉子，五官端正，颇有几分吉普赛人的模样，脸膛叫风吹日晒得黢黑，仿佛常年曝在阳光风雨、冰雹霜雪里；动作利落，头脑显然在常人之上，虽说一身粗布衣裳、头戴毛皮帽子，周身却透着一股说不清道不明的斯文与教养。他的嗓音腔调，布里尔顿方才已经留意到了；此刻哈伯勒向市长抬手碰了碰帽檐，他又留意到这人的一双手，虽然磨粗了、晒黑了，却生得周正而秀气。他身上的某种东西，他姿态中的某种东西，他眼波的一瞥，似乎都在表明：论身份气度，他高出那些正用琢磨的、略带困惑的目光打量他的警察一头——不论穿制服的还是穿便衣的。

===Original===

“Well, and what’s all this, now?” said Mallalieu coming to a halt and looking round. “What’s he got to say, like?”

The superintendent looked at Harborough and nodded. And Harborough took that nod at its true meaning, and he spoke﻿—readily.

“This!” he said, turning to the newcomers, and finally addressing himself to Mallalieu. “And it’s what I’ve already said to the superintendent here. I know nothing about what’s happened to Kitely. I know no more of his murder than you do﻿—not so much, I should say﻿—for I know naught at all beyond what I’ve been told. I left my house at eight o’clock last night﻿—I’ve been away all night﻿—I got back at six o’clock this morning. As soon as I heard what was afoot, I came straight here. I put it to you, Mr. Mayor﻿—if I’d killed this old man, do you think I’d have come back? Is it likely?”

===Chinese===

“那么，这到底是怎么一回事？”马拉柳站定下来，环顾四周，说道。“他有什么要说的没有？”

警司朝哈伯勒望了望，点了点头。哈伯勒也领会了这一点头的真意，随即开了口——痛痛快快地。

“就是这么回事！”他说着转向新来的人，末了面向马拉柳。“这话我也跟这儿的警司说过了。基特利出了什么事，我一概不知。他遇害的内情，我知道的不比你们多——依我说还不如你们——因为除了听人告诉我的那些，我压根一无所知。昨晚八点我离开家——一整夜都在外头——今天早上六点才回来。我一听说出了事，马上就直奔这儿来了。市长先生，我倒要请问您——要是我杀了那个老头儿，您想我还会回来吗？有这个道理吗？”

===Original===

“You might ha’ done, you know,” answered Mallalieu. “There’s no accounting for what folks will do﻿—in such cases. But﻿—what else? Say aught you like﻿—it’s all informal, this.”

“Very well,” continued Harborough. “They tell me the old man was strangled by a piece of cord that was evidently cut off one of my coils. Now, is there any man in his common senses would believe that if I did that job, I should leave such a bit of clear evidence behind me? I’m not a fool!”

“You might ha’ been interrupted before you could take that cord off his neck,” suggested Mallalieu.

“Aye﻿—but you’d have to reckon up the average chances of that!” exclaimed Harborough, with a sharp glance at the bystanders. “And the chances are in my favour. No, sir!﻿—whoever did this job, cut that length of cord off my coil, which anybody could get at, and used it to throw suspicion on me! That’s the truth﻿—and you’ll find it out some day, whatever happens now.”

===Chinese===

“你也可能回来嘛，这谁知道，”马拉柳答道。“这种案子里，人的行事没个准谱。不过——还有呢？想说什么尽管说——这又不是什么正式场合。”

“很好，”哈伯勒接着说。“他们告诉我，那老头儿是叫一截绳子勒死的，而那绳子分明是从我的绳卷上铰下来的。那么，但凡神志正常的男人，有谁会相信：我要是干了那件事，会把这么一件明明白白的罪证留在身后？我又不是傻子！”

“也兴许你还没来得及把绳子从他脖子上解下来，就被人打断了呀，”马拉柳提了一句。

“是啊——可那也得掂量掂量这种事摊开来讲有几分可能！”哈伯勒喊道，锐利地扫了旁观的人一眼。 “而这几分可能偏偏在我这边。不，先生！——不管是谁干的这事，都是从我那随便什么人都够得着的绳卷上，铰下那一截绳子，故意拿来把嫌疑栽到我头上！实情就是这样——不管眼下闹成什么样，总有一天你们会明白的。”

===Original===

Mallalieu exchanged glances with the superintendent and then faced Harborough squarely, with an air of inviting confidence.

“Now, my lad!” he said, almost coaxingly. “There’s a very simple thing to do, and it’ll clear this up as far as you’re concerned. Just answer a plain question. Where ha’ you been all night?”

A tense silence fell﻿—broken by the crackling of the wood in the grate, which the charwoman had at last succeeded in stirring into a blaze, and by the rattling of the fire-irons which she now arranged in the fender. Everybody was watching the suspected man, and nobody as keenly as Brereton. And Brereton saw that a deadlock was at hand. A strange look of obstinacy and hardness came into Harborough’s eyes, and he shook his head.

===Chinese===

马拉柳与警司交换了一个眼色，随即正面对着哈伯勒，摆出一副请人交心的神气。

“我说，小伙子！”他说，口气简直是在哄人。“有件顶简单的事，做了就能把你这一头的嫌疑洗刷干净。就答一个平平常常的问题。你一整夜都待在哪儿？”

一阵紧张的沉默落了下来——把它打破的，是炉膛里柴火的噼啪声，那清洁女工好歹把火拨得烧旺了；还有她此刻把火钩火铲归置进炉栏时弄出的叮当脆响。人人都盯着这个涉嫌的男人，谁也不及布里尔顿盯得紧。而布里尔顿看出，僵局就要来了。哈伯勒眼里浮起一种又倔又硬的异样神色，他摇了摇头。

===Original===

“No!” he answered. “I shan’t say! The truth’ll come out in good time without that. It’s not necessary for me to say. Where I was during the night is my business﻿—nobody else’s.”

“You’ll not tell?” asked Mallalieu.

“I shan’t tell,” replied Harborough.

“You’re in danger, you know,” said Mallalieu.

“In your opinion,” responded Harborough, doggedly. “Not in mine! There’s law in this country. You can arrest me, if you like﻿—but you’ll have your work set to prove that I killed yon old man. No, sir! But﻿—” here he paused, and looking round him, laughed almost maliciously “﻿—but I’ll tell you what I’ll do,” he went on. “I’ll tell you this, if it’ll do you any good﻿—if I liked to say the word, I could prove my innocence down to the ground! There!”

===Chinese===

“不！”他答道。“我不说！不靠这个，真相到时候自会水落石出。我没有必要说。我夜里在哪儿是我自己的事——与旁人无干。”

“你不肯说？”马拉柳问。

“我不说，”哈伯勒答。

“你知道，你这是在往险地里走，”马拉柳说。

“那是你们的看法，”哈伯勒执拗地答道。“不是我的！这个国家是有王法的。你们要愿意，尽可以逮捕我——可要证明是我杀了那个老头儿，你们可得下一番死工夫。不，先生！不过——”说到这里他顿住，环顾四周，笑得近乎不怀好意，“——不过我可以告诉你们我打算怎么办，”他接着说。“要是这话对你们有什么用处，我就说这么一句——只要我肯吐一个字，我就能把我的清白证个底朝天！就这样！”

===Original===

The constable who had come down from the Shawl with Bent and Brereton had for some time been endeavouring to catch the eye of the superintendent. Succeeding in his attempts at last, he beckoned that official into a quiet corner of the room, and turning his back on the group near the fireplace, pulled something out of his pocket. The two men bent over it, and the constable began to talk in whispers.

Mallalieu meanwhile was eyeing Harborough in his stealthy, steady fashion. He looked as if he was reckoning him up.

“Well, my lad,” he observed at last. “You’re making a mistake. If you can’t or won’t tell what you’ve been doing with yourself between eight last night and six this morning, why, then﻿—”

===Chinese===

随本特和布里尔顿从肖山下来的那名警察，有一阵子一直在想法子引警司看他。末了总算如愿，他把这位长官招到屋内一个僻静的角落，背朝着壁炉边的人群，从口袋里掏出一样东西。两个人俯身对着它，那警察开始低声耳语。

这工夫，马拉柳正用他那不动声色、稳稳当当的方式打量着哈伯勒，像是在心里盘算掂量这个人。

“我说，小伙子，”他末了开口道。“你这是在犯错误。要是你说不出、也不肯说你昨晚八点到今早六点之间自己干了什么，那么——”

===Original===

The superintendent came back, holding something in his hand. He, too, looked at Harborough.

“Will you hold up your left foot?﻿—turn the sole up,” he asked. “Just to see﻿—something.”

Harborough complied, readily, but with obvious scornful impatience. And when he had shown the sole of the left foot, the superintendent opened his hand and revealed a small crescent-shaped bit of bright steel.

“That’s off the toe of your boot, Harborough,” he said. “You know it is! And it’s been picked up﻿—just now, as it were﻿—where this affair happened. You must have lost it there during the last few hours, because it’s quite bright﻿—not a speck of rust on it, you see. What do you say to that, now?”

“Naught!” retorted Harborough, defiantly. “It is mine, of course﻿—I noticed it was working loose yesterday. And if it was picked up in that wood, what then? I passed through there last night on my way to﻿—where I was going. God﻿—you don’t mean to say you’d set a man’s life on bits o’ things like that!”

===Chinese===

警司回来了，手里攥着一样东西。他也望着哈伯勒。

“请你把左脚抬起来好不好？——鞋底翻上来，”他说。“只是看看——一样东西。”

哈伯勒照办了，倒很爽快，只是明显带着轻蔑的不耐烦。等他把左脚鞋底亮了出来，警司摊开手掌，露出一个月牙形的小钢片，亮闪闪的。

“这是从你靴子头上掉下来的，哈伯勒，”他说。“你自己清楚！它是在出事的那个地方捡到的——可以说，就是方才捡到的。这几小时里你准是在那儿弄丢的，因为它还锃亮——你看，一点锈斑也没有。这下你还有什么说的？”

“没什么说的！”哈伯勒反唇相讥，一副不服的架势。“它当然是我的——昨天我就发觉它松了。就算它是在那片林子里捡到的，又怎么样？我昨晚路过那儿，是在去——去我要去的地方的路上。老天——你们总不至于说，要拿这么些零碎玩意儿来定一个人的生死吧！”

===Original===

Mallalieu beckoned the superintendent aside and talked with him. Almost at once he himself turned away and left the room, and the superintendent came back to the group by the fireplace.

“Well, there’s no help for it, Harborough,” he said. “We shall have to detain you﻿—and I shall have to charge you, presently. It can’t be helped﻿—and I hope you’ll be able to clear yourself.”

“I expected nothing else,” replied Harborough. “I’m not blaming you﻿—nor anybody. Mr. Bent,” he continued, turning to where Bent and Brereton stood a little apart. “I’d be obliged to you if you’d do something for me. Go and tell my daughter about this, if you please! You see, I came straight down here﻿—I didn’t go into my house when I got back. If you’d just step up and tell her﻿—and bid her not be afraid﻿—there’s naught to be afraid of, as she’ll find﻿—as everybody’ll find.”

===Chinese===

马拉柳把警司招到一旁，同他交谈了几句。紧接着他自己便转身出了屋，警司回到壁炉边的人群那儿。

“唉，没法子了，哈伯勒，”他说。“我们只得把你拘起来——过一会儿，我还得对你正式提出控告。这是不得已的事——我希望你能把自己洗刷清楚。”

“我本来就没指望别的，”哈伯勒答道。“我不怪你——也不怪任何人。本特先生，”他接着说，转向站在稍远处的本特和布里尔顿。“要是你能替我办件事，我就承你的情了。麻烦你去把这件事告诉我女儿！你知道，我是直接到这儿来的——到家门口也没进屋。劳驾你走一趟告诉她——叫她别怕——没什么可怕的，她自己会发现——所有人都会发现的。”

===Original===

“Certainly,” said Bent. “I’ll go at once.” He tapped Brereton on the arm, and led him out into the street. “Well?” he asked, when they were outside. “What do you think of that, now?”

“That man gives one all the suggestion of innocence,” remarked Brereton, thoughtfully, “and from a merely superficial observation of him, I, personally, should say he is innocent. But then, you know, I’ve known the most hardened and crafty criminals assume an air of innocence, and keep it up, to the very end. However, we aren’t concerned about that just now﻿—the critical point here, for Harborough, at any rate, is the evidence against him.”

“And what do you think of that?” asked Bent.

“There’s enough to warrant his arrest,” answered Brereton, “and he’ll be committed on it, and he’ll go for trial. All that’s certain﻿—unless he’s a sensible man, and tells what he was doing with himself between eight and ten o’clock last night.”

===Chinese===

“当然可以，”本特说。“我这就去。”他碰了碰布里尔顿的胳膊，领他走到街上。“怎么样？”到了外面他问道。“现在你怎么看？”

“那个人浑身上下都透着无辜的意思，”布里尔顿若有所思地说，“单凭对他浮光掠影的观察，就我个人而言，我会说他是无辜的。可是你要知道，我见过最顽固、最狡诈的罪犯摆出一副无辜的架势，还能把这架势撑到底、撑到最后。不过，眼下咱们操心的不是这个——对哈伯勒来说，这里头的要害，无论如何在于对他不利的证据。”

“那你怎么看那些证据？”本特问。

“这些证据足够把他逮捕归案了，”布里尔顿答道，“凭这些，他会被移送庭审，会去受审。这些都逃不掉——除非他是个明白人，肯说出昨晚八点到十点之间他自己干了什么。”

===Original===

“Ah, and why doesn’t he?” said Bent. “He must have some good reason. I wonder if his daughter can persuade him?”

“Isn’t that his daughter coming towards us?” inquired Brereton.

Bent glanced along the road and saw Avice Harborough at a little distance, hastening in their direction and talking earnestly to a middle-aged man who was evidently listening with grave concern to what she said.

“Yes, that’s she,” he replied, “and that’s Northrop with her﻿—the man that Mallalieu was playing cards with last night. She’s governess to Northrop’s two younger children﻿—I expect she’s heard about her father, and has been to get Northrop to come down with her﻿—he’s a magistrate.”

===Chinese===

“唉，可他为什么不说呢？”本特说。“他一定有什么过硬的理由。他女儿能不能劝动他呢？”

“朝咱们走来的，那不是他女儿吗？”布里尔顿问道。

本特沿路望去，看见不远处正是艾维丝·哈伯勒，正朝他们这边匆匆赶来，一路同身旁一个中年男人急切地说着什么，那人显然正凝神听着，满脸的郑重关切。

“对，就是她，”他答道，“陪她一块儿的是诺思罗普——就是昨晚跟马拉柳打牌的那个人。她在诺思罗普家当家庭教师，教他那两个小些的孩子——我猜她已经听说了她父亲的事，是去请诺思罗普陪她一起来的——他是位治安法官。”

===Original===

Avice listened with ill-concealed impatience while Bent delivered his message. He twice repeated Harborough’s injunction that she was not to be afraid, and her impatience increased.

“I’m not afraid,” she answered. “That is, afraid of nothing but my father’s obstinacy! I know him. And I know that if he’s said he won’t tell anything about his whereabouts last night, he won’t! And if you want to help him﻿—as you seem to do﻿—you must recognize that.”

“Wouldn’t he tell you?” suggested Brereton.

The girl shook her head.

“Once or twice a year,” she answered, “he goes away for a night, like that, and I never know﻿—never have known﻿—where he goes. There’s some mystery about it﻿—I know there is. He won’t tell﻿—he’ll let things go to the last, and even then he won’t tell. You won’t be able to help him that way﻿—there’s only one way you can help.”

===Chinese===

本特转达口信时，艾维丝听着，那份不耐烦几乎藏不住。哈伯勒嘱咐叫她不必害怕，本特把这话重复了两遍，她的不耐烦反倒更重了。

“我不怕，”她答道。“就是说，除了我父亲的倔脾气，什么都不怕！我了解他。他既然说了不肯透露昨晚行踪的半个字，那就绝不会透露！你们要是想帮他——看样子你们是想帮的——就必须认清这一点。”

“连你也不告诉？”布里尔顿提了一句。

姑娘摇了摇头。

“一年总有一两回，”她答道，“他就那样出去一整夜，我从不知道——从来不知道——他去了哪儿。这里头有蹊跷——我知道有。他不会说的——他会任凭事情拖到最后一刻，到了那会儿他也不会说。走这条路你们帮不了他——要帮，只有一条路。”

===Original===

“What way?” asked Bent.

“Find the murderer!” exclaimed Avice with a quick flash of her eyes in Brereton’s direction. “My father is as innocent as I am﻿—find the man who did it and clear him that way. Don’t wait for what these police people do﻿—they’ll waste time over my father. Do something! They’re all on the wrong track﻿—let somebody get on the right one!”

“She’s right!” said Northrop, a shrewd-faced little man, who looked genuinely disturbed. “You know what police are, Mr. Bent﻿—if they get hold of one notion they’re deaf to all others. While they’re concentrating on Harborough, you know, the real man’ll be going free﻿—laughing in his sleeve, very like.”

“But﻿—what are we to do?” asked Bent. “What are we to start on?”

===Chinese===

“哪条路？”本特问。

“找到凶手！”艾维丝喊道，眼睛朝布里尔顿那边飞快地一闪。“我父亲跟我一样清白——找到干这事的人，用这个法子还他清白。别等着这些警察行事——他们只会在我父亲身上白耗工夫。行动起来呀！他们都走岔了道——总得有人走上正道才好！”

“她说得对！”诺思罗普说。他是个面目精明的小个子男人，看样子是真着了急。“警察是什么做派您是知道的，本特先生——他们抓住一个念头，别的就一概听不进去了。他们死盯着哈伯勒的这工夫，您想想，真凶可就逍遥自在去了——十有八九还在袖子里偷着乐呢。”

“可是——我们能做什么呢？”本特问。“从哪儿下手呢？”

===Original===

“Find out about Kitely himself!” exclaimed Avice. “Who knows anything about him? He may have had enemies﻿—he may have been tracked here. Find out if there was any motive!” She paused and looked half appealingly, half-searchingly at Brereton. “I heard you’re a barrister﻿—a clever one,” she went on, hesitating a little. “Can’t﻿—can’t you suggest anything?”

“There’s something I’ll suggest at once,” responded Brereton impulsively. “Whatever else is done, your father’s got to be defended. I’ll defend him﻿—to the best of my ability﻿—if you’ll let me﻿—and at no cost to him.”

“Well spoken, sir!” exclaimed Northrop. “That’s the style!”

“But we must keep to legal etiquette,” continued Brereton, smiling at the little man’s enthusiasm. “You must go to a solicitor and tell him to instruct me﻿—it’s a mere form. Mr. Bent will take you to his solicitor, and he’ll see me. Then I can appear in due form when they bring your father before the magistrates. Look here, Bent,” he went on, wishing to stop any expression of gratitude from the girl, “you take Miss Harborough to your solicitor﻿—if he isn’t up, rouse him out. Tell him what I propose to do, and make an appointment with him for me. Now run along, both of you﻿—I want to speak to this gentleman a minute.”

===Chinese===

“去查基特利本人的底细！”艾维丝喊道。“谁知道他的来历？他也许结过仇家——也许是被人一路追到这里来的。查清楚这里头有没有作案的动机！”她顿了顿，半是恳求、半是探询地望着布里尔顿。“我听说您是位出庭律师——还是有本事的一位，”她接着说，稍稍迟疑了一下。“您——您就不能出个主意吗？”

“有个主意我立刻就能出，”布里尔顿脱口应道。“不管还做什么，令尊总得有人辩护。我来给他辩护——尽我所能——只要你们肯——而且分文不取。”

“说得好，先生！”诺思罗普喊道。“要的就是这个气派！”

“不过咱们得按法律上的规矩来，”布里尔顿接着说，冲着这小个子男人的热乎劲儿笑了笑。“你得先去见一位事务律师，让他来正式委托我——不过走个形式。本特先生会带你去他的事务律师那儿，那位自会来见我。到时候他们把令尊提到治安法官面前，我就能正正式式出庭了。我说，本特，”他又道，一心要拦住姑娘的道谢，“你带哈伯勒小姐去你那位事务律师家——他要是还没起床，就把他叫起来。把我的打算告诉他，再替我跟他约个时间。好了，你们俩这就去吧——我要跟这位先生说一分钟话。”

===Original===

He took Northrop’s arm, turned him in the direction of the Shawl, walked him a few paces, and then asked him a direct question.

“Now, what do you know of this man Harborough?”

“He’s a queer chap﻿—a mystery man, sir,” answered Northrop. “A sort of jack-of-all-trades. He’s a better sort﻿—you’d say, to hear him talk, he’d been a gentleman. You can see what his daughter is﻿—he educated her well. He’s means of some sort﻿—apart from what he earns. Yes, there’s some mystery about that man, sir﻿—but I’ll never believe he did this job. No, sir!”

“Then we must act on the daughter’s suggestion and find out who did,” observed Brereton. “There is as much mystery about that as about Harborough.”

“All mystery, sir!” agreed Northrop. “It’s odd﻿—I came through them woods on the Shawl there about a quarter to ten last night: I’d been across to the other side to see a man of mine that’s poorly in bed. Now, I never heard aught, never saw aught﻿—but then, it’s true I was hurrying﻿—I’d made an appointment for a hand at whist with the Mayor at my house at ten o’clock, and I thought I was late. I never heard a sound﻿—not so much as a dead twig snap! But then, it would ha’ been before that﻿—at some time.”

===Chinese===

他挽起诺思罗普的胳膊，把他转向肖山的方向，走了几步，然后直截了当地问了他一个问题。

“这么说，这位哈伯勒，您知道他的什么底细？”

“他是个怪人——一个来历不明的人，先生，”诺思罗普答道。“就是那种样样活计都拿得起来的角色。他这人档次不低——听他谈吐，您会说他做过体面人。他女儿什么样您也看见了——他把她调教得很好。他还有些来路——不单靠挣的那点钱。是啊，这个人身上是有蹊跷，先生——可我绝不会相信这事是他干的。不会的，先生！”

“那咱们就得照他女儿的主意办，查出真凶是谁，”布里尔顿说。“这桩事本身的谜，一点也不比哈伯勒这个人身上的少。”

“全是谜，先生！”诺思罗普附和道。“说来也怪——昨晚九点三刻前后，我穿过肖山上的那片树林子：我是到对面去看我的一个伙计，他病倒在床上。您知道，我什么也没听见，什么也没看见——不过说实在的，我那会儿正赶路——我约好十点钟在家里陪市长打一场惠斯特牌，还当自己迟到了呢。我一点动静也没听见——连一根枯树枝折断的声儿都没有！不过话说回来，那事准是在那之前——在哪个时候干的。”

===Original===

“Yes, at some time,” agreed Brereton. “Well﻿—I’ll see you in court, no doubt.”

He turned back, and followed Bent and Avice at a distance, watching them thoughtfully.

“At some time?” he mused. “Um! Well, I’m now conversant with the movements of two inhabitants of Highmarket at a critical period of last night. Mallalieu didn’t go to cards with Northrop until ten o’clock, and at ten o’clock Cotherstone returned to his house after being absent﻿—one hour.”

===Chinese===

“对，在哪个时候，”布里尔顿附和道。“那么——咱们法庭上见，错不了。”

他转身往回走，远远跟着本特和艾维丝，若有所思地望着他们。

“在哪个时候？”他暗自琢磨。“唔！好啊，海马基特有两位居民在昨夜那个要命的时段里的行踪，我眼下算是摸清楚了。马拉柳直到十点钟才去诺思罗普家打牌；而十点钟整，科瑟斯通离家在外一个钟头之后，回了自己的家。”

## IX. Antecedents / 九　来历

===Original===
During the interval which elapsed between these early morning proceedings and the bringing up of Harborough before the borough magistrates in a densely-packed court, Brereton made up his mind as to what he would do. He would act on Avice Harborough’s suggestion, and, while watching the trend of affairs on behalf of the suspected man, would find out all he could about the murdered one. At that moment﻿—so far as Brereton knew﻿—there was only one person in Highmarket who was likely to know anything about Kitely: that person, of course, was the queer-looking housekeeper. He accordingly determined, even at that early stage of the proceedings, to have Miss Pett in the witness-box.

Harborough, who had been formally arrested and charged by the police after the conversation at the police-station, was not produced in court until eleven o’clock, by which time the whole town and neighbourhood were astir with excitement. Somewhat to Brereton’s surprise, the prosecuting counsel, who had been hastily fetched from Norcaster and instructed on the way, went more fully into the case than was usual. Brereton had expected that the police would ask for an adjournment after the usual evidence of the superficial facts, and of the prisoner’s arrest, had been offered; instead of that, the prosecution brought forward several witnesses, and amongst them the bank-manager, who said that when he cashed Kitely’s draft for him the previous morning, in Harborough’s presence, he gave Kitely the one half of the money in gold. The significance of this evidence immediately transpired: a constable succeeded the bank-manager and testified that after searching the prisoner after his arrest he found on him over twenty pounds in sovereigns and half-sovereigns, placed in a wash-leather bag.

Brereton immediately recognized the impression which this evidence made. He saw that it weighed with the half-dozen solid and slow-thinking men who sat on one side or the other of Mallalieu on the magisterial bench; he felt the atmosphere of suspicion which it engendered in the court. But he did nothing: he had already learned sufficient from Avice in a consultation with her and Bent’s solicitor to know that it would be very easy to prove to a jury that it was no unusual thing for Harborough to carry twenty or thirty pounds in gold on him. Of all these witnesses Brereton asked scarcely anything﻿—but he made it clear that when Harborough was met near his cottage at daybreak that morning by two constables who informed him of what had happened, he expressed great astonishment, jeered at the notion that he had had anything to do with the murder, and, without going on to his own door, offered voluntarily to walk straight to the police-station.

But when Miss Pett﻿—who had discarded her red and yellow turban, and appeared in rusty black garments which accentuated the old-ivory tint of her remarkable countenance﻿—had come into the witness-box and answered a few commonplace questions as to the dead man’s movements on the previous evening, Brereton prepared himself for the episode which he knew to be important. Amidst a deep silence﻿—something suggesting to everybody that Mr. Bent’s sharp-looking London friend was about to get at things﻿—he put his first question to Miss Pett.

===Chinese===
从清晨那几桩事情，到哈伯勒被押上法庭、在挤得水泄不通的庭上面见自治市的治安法官们，中间隔着一段时光，布里尔顿便趁着这段时间拿定了主意，知道自己该怎么做。他要照艾维丝·哈伯勒的主意行事：一面替这位涉嫌犯罪的人盯住事态的走向，一面把遇害者本人的底细尽量查个明白。据布里尔顿所知，此时此刻的海马基特，可能对基特利略知一二的只有一个人——这个人，自然就是那位模样古怪的女管家。于是他打定主意，哪怕程序才刚刚开了个头，也要把佩特小姐传上证人席。

在警察局里的那番谈话之后，哈伯勒已被警方正式逮捕并加以控告，直到十一点钟才被提到堂上，此时全镇上下连同四乡八邻都已沸腾起来。让布里尔顿有些意外的是，那位从诺卡斯特（Norcaster）匆匆请来、在路上才听完案情交代的控方律师，把这桩案子问得比寻常更细。布里尔顿原以为，照例摆过那些浮皮潦草的事实经过连同逮捕被告的证词之后，警方会请求延期审理；不料控方却接连传唤了几名证人，其中便有那位银行经理。经理作证说，前一天上午他给基特利兑付汇票时——哈伯勒当时在场——他把那笔钱的一半付给了基特利，付的是金币。这份证词的分量立刻显露出来：紧随银行经理之后，一名警员出庭作证，说逮捕被告之后对其搜身，从他身上搜出二十多镑的沙弗林金币和半沙弗林金币，装在一只麂皮小钱袋里。

布里尔顿当即看出了这份证词造成的影响。他看得出，它打动了治安法官席上分坐在马拉柳两侧的那六位敦实而脑筋迟缓的人物；他感觉到它在法庭里催生出的那股疑忌气氛。但他按兵不动：他先前在与艾维丝及本特的律师会商时已经了解得足够清楚，知道要让陪审团相信，哈伯勒身上带着二三十镑金币本是家常便饭，易如反掌。对这些证人，布里尔顿几乎没有多问什么——只有一点他问得明明白白：那天破晓时分，两名警员在哈伯勒的村舍附近撞见他，把出了什么事告知于他，他闻言大为惊讶，对说自己同凶案有牵连的说法嗤之以鼻，而且连自家门口都没有再进，便主动提出径直上警察局去。

待到佩特小姐走进证人席——她已摘下那顶红黄相间的头巾帽，换上一身乌暗发锈的黑衣裳，把那张惹眼面孔的老象牙色衬得愈发分明——照例答过几句关于死者头天晚上行踪的寻常问话之后，布里尔顿便为这场他自知干系重大的交锋做好了准备。法庭上一片深寂——那气氛让人人都料到，本特先生那位模样精明的伦敦朋友就要问到点子上了——他向佩特小姐抛出了第一个问题。

===Original===
“How long have you known Mr. Kitely?”

“Ever since I engaged with him as his housekeeper,” answered Miss Pett.

“How long since is that?” asked Brereton.

“Nine to ten years﻿—nearly ten.”

“You have been with him, as housekeeper, nearly ten years﻿—continuously?”

“Never left him since I first came to him.”

===Chinese===
“您认识基特利先生多久了？”

“打我受雇给他当女管家那天起，”佩特小姐答道。

“那是多久以前的事？”布里尔顿问。

“九到十年——快满十年了。”

“您给他当女管家，当了将近十年——中间从没间断过？”

“打我头一回进他家的门，就没离开过他。”

===Original===
“Where did you first come to him﻿—where did he live then?”

“In London.”

“Yes﻿—and where, in London?”

“83, Acacia Grove, Camberwell.”
